================================================================================
BANNERWAKE — MOD DESIGN DOCUMENT
For use with Claude Code. This is the source of truth.
If it is not in this document, it does not ship without explicit approval.
Version: 2.4 — hostile local v1.4.5 feasibility and engine audit pass
================================================================================

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 1: CORE PHILOSOPHY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Bannerwake respects what Bannerlord is, but is not afraid to rebuild what it
should have been. Where vanilla falls short of its own vision, Bannerwake
finishes the thought. This is a private standalone overhaul, not a compatibility
mod. It is for personal use only, drawing assets from other mods privately.

Campaign mode assumption: NO DEATH, NO BIRTH. No mechanics depend on killing
heirs or waiting for births. Never implement anything that does.

NPC / DIALOGUE REFERENCE LOCK:
The authoritative local v1.4.5 character and dialogue reference is:
  C:\Users\korom\Documents\MOUNT AND BLADE BANNERLORD PRIVATE MOD\
  BANNERWAKE_ALL_NPC_AND_DIALOGUE_REFERENCE_FOR_CLAUDE.md

Use it as hard canon for vanilla/War Sails lord IDs, clans, kingdoms, ages,
traits, XML voice labels, family links, hero existence, and starting holdings.
Dialogue is persona/tag/state driven, not a bespoke transcript attached to
each lord. Bannerwake-authored lord dialogue may be written from XML voice
labels and traits, but sample lines are design writing unless a specific
vanilla string ID is cited.

CORRECTION LOCKS:
- No extracted lord Hero or NPCCharacter record has a pregnancy marker.
  Zorina and Brighan are NOT XML-confirmed pregnant. No quests, dialogue,
  timers, births, succession logic, or pregnancy-state notes may depend on that
  old claim.
- Taorse [lord_5_21_2] is an active fen Eingal noble: age 18, female,
  softspoken, honorable, daughter of Carfyd and Beathag. She is not the
  ancient Grovebound elder. Grovebound elder content must use a Bannerwake-
  invented name.
- Branoc [lord_5_18] is an active fen Morcar noble, spouse of Seonag and son
  of Pryndor. Do not use Branoc as a Grovebound leader name.
- Aldric [lord_4_3] is vanilla dey Tihr. Do not use Aldric as the Company of
  the Golden Boar leader name.
- Culharn [lord_5_9] remains active fen Uvain unless an explicit authored
  defection/conversion event changes him.
- Hongard Castle [castle_V2] belongs to dey Jelind/Vartin in vanilla local
  XML. Vrak taking Hongard displaces Vartin; it is not an independent castle.
- Mortia damages four Vlandian clans directly: dey Meroc, dey Tihr,
  dey Arromanc, and dey Jelind.

LOCAL ENGINE FEASIBILITY LOCK — 2026-06-05:
Target install is Mount & Blade II: Bannerlord beta v1.4.5 with official
NavalDLC / War Sails v1.2.5 loaded. The local NavalDLC SubModule.xml requires
base v1.4.5 and loads campaign XML for ships, ship hulls, ship slots, ship
upgrade pieces, naval party templates, naval cultures, kingdoms, clans, heroes,
settlements, ports, shipyards, naval equipment, naval lords, naval characters,
and XSLT transforms. Bannerwake must extend these local systems instead of
recreating them blindly.

Code-facing implementation boundaries:
- XML can define cultures, clans/factions, kingdoms, heroes, NPCCharacters,
  party templates, items, equipment rosters, settlements, ship data, banners,
  text, and scenes. XML alone cannot run Bannerwake's dynamic state machine,
  karma, event cards, custom AI vows, land seizures, port blockades, economic
  timers, Ghilman contract voids, Golden Boar memory, Shroud fragments, or
  Vrak/Black Keel re-emergence.
- Campaign logic belongs in C# CampaignBehaviorBase classes with saved state
  through SyncData. Use CampaignEvents such as DailyTick, HourlyTick,
  DailyTickClan, DailyTickParty, DailyTickSettlement, OnSettlementEntered,
  Before/AfterSettlementEntered, OnSettlementOwnerChanged, OnPlayerBattleEnd,
  OnClanChangedKingdom, WarDeclared/MakePeace, OnBlockadeActivated/
  OnBlockadeDeactivated, OnShipCreated/Destroyed/OwnerChanged/Repaired, and
  mobile-party naval navigation/raft state events where applicable.
- Do not write fake API names as if they are confirmed. If an event is desired
  but not locally confirmed, describe it as custom polling or a Bannerwake
  wrapper, not a vanilla hook.
- Settlement ownership changes must use TaleWorlds action APIs such as
  ChangeOwnerOfSettlementAction.ApplyByDefault/ApplyBySiege/ApplyByRebellion
  or a deliberately chosen adjacent ApplyBy... method. Do not directly mutate
  owner fields in gameplay code. Kingdom creation/defection/mercenary status
  must use ChangeKingdomAction methods.
- War/peace state must use DeclareWarAction and MakePeaceAction or kingdom
  decision paths, not silent relation edits.
- Party AI promises require DefaultMobilePartyAIModel replacement, Harmony
  patches, or faction-specific behavior gates. XML party templates can shape
  parties, but they cannot enforce "never raids villages," "never breaks a
  contract," "avoids open battle," or "voids contract before Ghilman-vs-Ghilman."
- Wages and recruitment cost are controlled through the wage/recruitment model
  surface, locally visible as DefaultPartyWageModel methods such as
  GetCharacterWage and GetTotalWage. Do not invent or reference a fake
  troop-wage API method name.
- Weather/naval danger should build on DefaultMapWeatherModel,
  DefaultCampaignShipDamageModel, ShipHelper, PortStateHelper,
  DefaultSettlementPatrolModel, ship/port XML, and NavalDLC blockade/ship
  events. Storms, winter-sea asymmetry, port restrictions, and ship damage are
  feasible, but they are not automatic faction behavior without code.
- Local minor factions are Faction records with is_minor_faction="true" and,
  usually, four minor_faction_character_templates. The old "hard cap of three
  named heroes" claim is false as an engine statement. Three captains may be a
  design budget, not a local engine limit.
- Ordinary vanilla minor-faction loops are not built for permanent settlement
  ownership. A sub-faction that owns land long-term must be promoted to a
  pseudo-kingdom/full kingdom structure or receive equivalent C# handling for
  ownership, garrisons, clans, parties, diplomacy, save/load, AI protection,
  encyclopedia state, and recovery.
- Campaign mode assumes NO DEATH and NO BIRTH as a Bannerwake design dependency.
  Local APIs include birth/death events, but Bannerwake must not depend on them.
- Armor color is conditional. Many vanilla body armor items support
  UseTeamColor; NavalDLC items are mixed, with many items lacking the flag.
  Use native dynamic color sections where supported. Otherwise create bw_
  material/texture variants and normalize texture/shading quality manually.
- "Fire" is partly supported by vanilla/War Sails surfaces such as fire pots,
  fireships, fire ballista, ship fire damage, and OnShipBurned-style mission
  events. Undying Flame infantry fire arrows, burning thrown weapons, and
  burning melee weapons remain custom mission/combat behavior until proven by
  source code.
- Battania's Ca fál, yew-branch sacrifice, and Dunglanys grove layer is
  Bannerwake design canon unless a vanilla string ID is cited. Local XML search
  did not confirm those names/phrases as literal vanilla strings.
- External mods in the reference library are research targets only unless their
  source code has been read against v1.4.5/War Sails. Mod descriptions, Nexus
  pages, and Steam descriptions are not implementation proof.

THE EIGHT PILLARS:

1. RESPECT AND REBUILD
   Vanilla players should still recognize Calradia. But everything must feel
   more intentional, more alive, more dangerous.

2. VISUAL IDENTITY FIRST
   Troops must look like they belong to their culture, role, and tier. Silhouette
   must communicate who they are and how dangerous they are. Armor chosen by
   visual fit, not item ID.

3. LORE AS MECHANICS
   Faction history, beliefs, rivalries, traditions must affect troops, battles,
   events, recruitment, diplomacy, economy, and campaign behavior. Lore is not
   flavor text. It is the design brief.

4. COHERENT DIFFICULTY
   Enemies are dangerous because they use their strengths — equipment, terrain,
   morale, formation, party composition. Not inflated stats.

5. NO ASSET DUMPING
   Every armor, weapon, unit, event, and feature must serve the world. Nothing
   added because it is available. Everything earns its place.

6. WAR SAILS MATTER
   The sea is a real strategic layer. Dangerous coasts, pirates, raiders, ports,
   fleets, marine troops, coastal politics, threats from the water. Players who
   ignore the sea suffer for it.

7. EARNED PROGRESSION
   Elite troops, rare equipment, powerful allies, meaningful victories must feel
   like achievements. Nothing trivially available.

8. GROUNDED TONE
   Historically inspired and immersive. Low-fantasy elements only as rare myths,
   relics, elite traditions, or late-game threats. The world is grounded. Danger
   can feel unnatural. The explanation never comes easily.

EMOTIONAL TARGET — the player's first session should contain:
- Troops that look like real soldiers with a culture behind them
- An enemy party that makes them hesitate (they don't know what it is)
- A faction event that feels reactive, not random
- A coastal approach that makes them realize the sea is a threat, not scenery

FANTASY CALIBRATION:
- No magic systems. No glowing weapons as baseline. No fantasy races.
- Relics and myths exist. Rare, hard to find, power always ambiguous.
- Late-game threats escalate into something genuinely terrifying — coherent,
  lore-grounded, in the Prophesy of Pendor tradition.
- The wrongness of Black Keel is the ceiling. Nothing more overt than that.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 1b: FEATURE FEASIBILITY MATRIX — LOCAL v1.4.5 / NAVALDLC
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This matrix is the implementation truth table. If later prose sounds more
absolute than this section, this section wins.

CORE STATE SYSTEM:
  Feasible: yes, high risk.
  Surface: C# CampaignBehaviorBase, SyncData, CampaignEvents, custom manager
    classes, saved per-sub-faction state, cooldowns, thresholds, and recovery
    timers.
  Not XML-only. Build empty skeleton first, test save/load, then add one
    faction at a time.

MAJOR FACTION LORE EVENTS:
  Feasible: yes.
  Surface: C# daily/hourly ticks, OnSettlementOwnerChanged, WarDeclared,
    MakePeace, clan/kingdom strength calculations, settlement-count trackers,
    battle outcomes, prosperity/security data, and authored event cards.
  Risk: event spam and contradictory triggers. Every event needs cooldown,
    priority, and "already fired in this world state" flags.

SUB-FACTION SPAWNING:
  Feasible: yes.
  Surface: native party templates, home settlements, bandit/mercenary party
    creation logic, NavalDLC naval party templates, and state gates.
  No separate spawn-budget layer. Use state gates, party templates, timers,
    and regional caps baked into the behavior classes.

PSEUDO-KINGDOM / CUSTOM-KINGDOM LANDHOLDERS:
  Feasible: yes, high risk.
  Applies to: Mortia/Vrak and Black Keel when landholding; possibly Last
    Legion, Ironjarls, Beni Zilal, Karakhergit, Jawwal in escalated cases.
  Surface: kingdom/clan XML, ChangeKingdomAction, ChangeOwnerOfSettlementAction,
    DeclareWarAction, MakePeaceAction, custom garrison setup, AI protection,
    owner notifications, save/load validation, encyclopedia integration, and
    recapture/re-emergence behavior.
  Do not implement a long-term landholder as an ordinary one-clan minor faction
    and expect vanilla to carry the design.

MORTIA / VRAK:
  Feasible: yes as custom kingdom/pseudo-kingdom.
  Surface: 4 clans, 24 named heroes, 5 starting/early seized settlements,
    custom kingdom or pseudo-kingdom records, Vlandian relations, custom AI,
    escape/re-emergence flags, and Vrak-specific dialogue/event memory.
  No death dependency. Vrak is defeated, routed, captured temporarily, exposed,
    or forced to escape. He never needs to die for the system to resolve.

BLACK KEEL:
  Feasible: yes as custom threat layer, not as vanilla pirates renamed.
  Local hooks: NavalDLC has northern_pirates, southern_pirates, pirate items,
    ship systems, ship combat, and sea-raider/corsair language. It does NOT
    have a Black Keel faction.
  Surface: custom pseudo-kingdom/clans for Hrothgar/captains, pirate spawn
    behavior, naval parties, Hvalvik seizure, face/armor reskins, event
    fragments, and no-definitive-answer writing rules.

NAVAL STRATEGY:
  Feasible: yes.
  Local hooks: NavalDLC ships, ship hulls, ship slots, ship upgrades, ports,
    shipyards, port scenes, navalraid village scenes, naval party templates,
    shipyard workers, blockades, wind/storm/seaworthiness/ship-damage surfaces.
  Surface: ShipHelper, PortStateHelper, DefaultMapWeatherModel,
    DefaultCampaignShipDamageModel, DefaultSettlementPatrolModel, campaign
    blockade/ship events, settlement economy behavior, and party AI.
  Risk: assuming War Sails automatically gives faction-specific strategy. It
    gives the layer; Bannerwake must write the doctrine.

PORT ECONOMY / BLOCKADE / TRIBUTE:
  Feasible: yes.
  Surface: settlement economy model/behavior, port/shipyard XML, blockade
    events, PortStateHelper restricted/trade states, caravan/ship route
    trackers, patrol spawns, settlement security/prosperity modifiers.
  Risk: hidden economy snowball. All port modifiers need caps and diagnostics.

FACTION-SPECIFIC PARTY AI:
  Feasible: yes, high risk.
  Applies to: Woodsmen not raiding villages, Battanians avoiding open plains,
    Jomsvikingr contract code, Ghilman avoiding Ghilman, Ironjarls only hitting
    political targets, Vrak not abandoning his fortress, Black Keel behaving as
    naval predators.
  Surface: DefaultMobilePartyAIModel replacement/Harmony patches, party
    component logic, target filters, campaign events, and fail-safe cleanup.
  XML party templates define troops; they do not enforce vows.

KARMA:
  Feasible: yes.
  Surface: saved C# variables, event outcomes, dialogue conditions, relation
    modifiers, prisoner/mercy actions, settlement reactions, and hidden state.
  No visible morality meter unless explicitly approved later.

EVENT CARDS / D&D ENCOUNTERS:
  Feasible: yes, high writing cost.
  Surface: authored text assets, campaign behavior triggers, consequence flags,
    cooldowns, region/season/state/karma weighting, settlement entry, travel
    polling, battle end, naval events, and delayed consequences.
  Risk: content scale. Hundreds of authored cards are minimum; thousands of
    variations if every modifier layer gets bespoke prose.

SHROUD / UNNAMED FLEET:
  Feasible: yes as authored conspiracy/endgame layer.
  Surface: fragments, hidden flags, custom parties, late-game fleet spawn,
    dialogue/encounter events, naval map behavior, and player-power threshold.
  "Omniscience" must be implemented as recorded player actions and world-state
    queries. The game cannot reference what was never logged.

UNDYING FLAME FIRE:
  Feasible: partial local support, custom infantry work required.
  Local hooks: fire pots, fireships, fire ballista, ship fire damage, burning
    ship events, fire-related NavalDLC strings.
  Custom required: infantry fire arrows, burning thrown weapons, burning melee
    weapon visuals/damage, AI volley behavior, and Flame-only gating.

BATTANIAN SACRED LAW / GROVEBOUND:
  Feasible: yes as authored lore + custom law/relation/crime behavior.
  Surface: custom sacred-site records, Grovebound parties/NPCs, protected
    Groveseer state, relationship penalties, event waves, and Gathering
    temporary party creation.
  Local XML does not confirm Ca fál/yew-sacrifice strings. Treat as Bannerwake
    lore unless a source ID is added.

ASERAI CARAVAN DISRUPTION:
  Feasible: yes.
  Surface: caravan destruction tracker, Aserai economy pressure, party spawn
    strength, recruitment/wage modifiers, town prosperity/security, and delayed
    army-strength effects.
  The ~60-day delay is a balance target. It must be tuned, not trusted.

TROOP TREES / MARINES / CULTURE LOCKS:
  Feasible: yes.
  Surface: NPCCharacter XML, upgrade paths, skill sets, equipment rosters,
    party templates, culture recruitment, coastal settlement checks, and
    NavalDLC marine/naval troop hooks.
  Risk: names and visuals can be done in XML; lord party composition doctrine
    needs C# reinforcement.

VISUAL REWORK:
  Feasible: yes, art-heavy.
  Surface: item XML, equipment rosters, .dds texture edits, material variants,
    mesh/material assignments, UseTeamColor where supported, and bw_ duplicate
    assets where not supported.
  Risk: texture quality mismatch. Bannerlord/NavalDLC assets are inconsistent;
    every final troop item needs visual QA, not just stat QA.

EXTERNAL MOD STUDY:
  Feasible: yes as research.
  Surface: source-code audit only. No external mod becomes a dependency until
    its v1.4.5/War Sails behavior is proven and conflict-tested.
  Private asset use remains private; maintain internal credit notes.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 2: FACTION COLORS (design palette + local XML implementation colors)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Legacy mood palette from Bannerlord faction art and wiki references
(NOT armor implementation colors):
- Aserai:          #B57A1E  (warm gold-bronze)
- Battania:        #284E19  (deep forest green)
- Khuzait:         #429081  (teal-grey)
- Northern Empire: #793191  (mid purple)
- Southern Empire: #382188  (deep purple-indigo)
- Sturgia:         #224277  (dark navy blue)
- Vlandia:         #8D291A  (deep crimson)
- Western Empire:  #591645  (dark burgundy-purple)

Local v1.4.5 XML implementation colors are authoritative for code:
- Aserai kingdom color/color2:          FF965228 / FF4F2212
- Battania kingdom color/color2:        FF2D3F1D / FFBFCBB0
- Khuzait kingdom color/color2:         FF468C7C / FFCCBB89
- Northern Empire kingdom color/color2: FF39223F / FFDE9953
- Southern Empire kingdom color/color2: FF9382D0 / FFDE9953
- Sturgia kingdom color/color2:         FF1C2A50 / FF949CCC
- Vlandia kingdom color/color2:         FF5C2017 / FFECBA44
- Western Empire kingdom color/color2:  FF9E5072 / FFDE9953
- Nord kingdom color/color2:            FF202931 / FFb7623c

ARMOR / CLOTH / SHIELD COLOR RULE:
- Troop armor, shield paint, cloth panels, cloaks, banners, and color-dynamic
  material sections match the unit's kingdom color/color2.
- Prefer Bannerlord's native dynamic color sections whenever an armor or cloth
  asset supports them. If a troop changes kingdom, those dynamic sections should
  resolve to that kingdom's colors.
- If an important asset lacks color-dynamic sections, create a `bw_` texture
  variant or material assignment that matches the intended kingdom color/color2.
- The mood palette above is only an art-reference note. It must not override
  local XML colors in troop implementation.
- No unit should wear another faction's colors without explicit lore reason
  (deserters, pirates, mixed-culture troops, captured equipment).

Nords (War Sails DLC): armor, shields, cloth, and dynamic armor sections match
  Nord kingdom colors FF202931 / FFb7623c. Do not use the old earthy
  brown/forest green/orange-red note as implementation guidance.
Pirates: source pirates may vary by origin and kit.
Black Keel: dedicated dark salt-weathered palette — tar black, charcoal grey,
  dead sea-brown, corroded iron, ashen cloth, and no clean bright metal. Black
  Keel has its own visual identity even if some source pirate assets do not.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 3: MAJOR FACTION IDENTITIES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

STURGIA — Kievan Rus Principality
  Cultural inspiration: Kievan Rus with Varangian Norse elite integration.
  Slavic at the base, Norse at the top. Grand Prince Raganvad.
  Visual: Sturgia XML colors FF1C2A50 / FF949CCC on armor dynamic sections,
          shields, cloth, and banners; iron grey, fur trim, and high-tier
          brocade as secondary materials. Round shields with Slavic and Norse
          knotwork. Peaked gilded helmets at high tier.
  Military: shieldwall infantry core. High morale, refuses retreat. Cavalry on
            flanks. Holds ground until it costs them everything.
  Naval: longships are their trade highway. Sturgian coasts are launch points.
  Lore mechanic: Boyar Council. Internal politics events fire on military losses.

VLANDIA — Norman Feudal Kingdom
  Cultural inspiration: Norman feudal Europe. King Derthert.
  Visual: Vlandia XML colors FF5C2017 / FFECBA44 on armor dynamic sections,
          shields, cloth, and banners. Kite and heater shields with lion.
          Heavy plate at elite tier. Every unit belongs to someone's banner.
  Military: heavy cavalry charge with crossbow suppression. Disrupt the line
            and Vlandia bleeds. Infantry holds, cavalry decides.
  Naval: amphibious invasion specialists. Their galleys carry knights. A Vlandian
         naval landing is not a raid — it is a conquest.
  Lore mechanic: Feudal Obligation. Lord loyalty to Derthert tracked. Extended
                 campaigns strain it. Weak kings lose vassals.

THE EMPIRE (all three factions)
  Cultural inspiration: Late Roman / early Byzantine.
  Visual: each Empire variant uses its local XML colors on dynamic armor
          sections, shields, cloth, and banners; silver scale, crested helmets,
          and eagle shields remain the shared Imperial material language.
    Northern:  FF39223F / FFDE9953
    Southern:  FF9382D0 / FFDE9953
    Western:   FF9E5072 / FFDE9953
  Military: formation warfare. Spear lines, archers, flanking cavalry. Hard to
            rout cleanly. Wins by not making mistakes.
  Naval: harbor control and supply chain warfare. Economic, not raiding.
  Lore mechanic: Imperial Legitimacy. Three claimants, one throne. Territory
                 triggers legitimacy crisis events.

BATTANIA — Celtic Highlands
  Cultural inspiration: Pictish, Irish, Welsh Celtic. King Caladog.
  Religion: Ca fál — Bannerwake-authored Battanian religious layer unless a
            vanilla string ID is later cited. Current design canon: two primary
            gods, sacred groves, Holy Men who travel freely by ancient law,
            ancestor-speaking through sacred lake water, and yew-branch human
            sacrifice at the grove outside Dunglanys. Local XML search did not
            confirm "Ca fál," the yew sacrifice, or the Dunglanys grove as
            literal vanilla strings; keep the layer, but do not call it
            XML-confirmed vanilla evidence.
  Visual: Battania XML colors FF2D3F1D / FFBFCBB0 on armor dynamic sections,
          shields, cloth, and banners; grey linen and dark iron as secondary
          materials. Celtic knotwork on shields and cloaks. Woad at elite tier.
          Lean silhouettes
          — a Battanian does not look armored because they don't want to be seen.
  Military: ambush and terrain exploitation. Lethal in forests and broken ground.
            Vulnerable on open plains. Desired AI behavior: avoid open pitched
            battle unless advantaged. This requires MobilePartyAI/model or
            Harmony behavior work, not XML-only troop design.
  Naval: river raiders and estuarine harassment. Fast shallow vessels. They
         disappear into waterways and strike when unwatched.
  Lore mechanic: Grove Councils. Slow to commit, unbreakable once committed.
                 Sacred sites on the map carry gameplay weight.

ASERAI — Desert Sultanate
  Cultural inspiration: Arab caliphate and Bedouin. Sultan Unqid.
  Visual: Aserai XML colors FF965228 / FF4F2212 on armor dynamic sections,
          shields, cloth, and banners; white linen over mail and brass metals
          as secondary materials. Turbaned helmets. Round calligraphic shields.
  Military: mounted harassment into decisive cavalry charge. Composite archers
            thin formations, mamluk cavalry finishes them. Poor in sieges,
            exceptional in open mobile warfare.
  Naval: merchant-raider duality. Dhow fleets. Southern sea lanes are Aserai
         territory in all but name.
  Lore mechanic: Caravan Economy. Destroying Aserai caravans systematically
                 weakens their armies within ~60 in-game days through a custom
                 C# disruption tracker tied to economy, party spawning, and
                 wage/recruitment pressure. Do not treat this as a vanilla
                 automatic consequence of caravan loss.

KHUZAIT — Steppe Khanate
  Cultural inspiration: Mongol and central Asian steppe. Monchug Khan.
  Visual: Khuzait XML colors FF468C7C / FFCCBB89 on armor dynamic sections,
          shields, cloth, and banners; lamellar with steppe aesthetic and
          horse-hair plumes. Small round shields. On a ship, they look wrong.
          Intentional.
  Military: swarm, feign retreat, encircle. Cannot be caught or pinned. Weak in
            sustained sieges and narrow terrain.
  Naval: NONE. River boats and ferry crossings only. Cannot threaten by sea.
         This strategic fact must be felt by the player.
  Lore mechanic: Khan's Unity. Khuzait power tied to internal cohesion. Military
                 weakness triggers clan fragmentation events.

NORDS — Kingdom of Nordvyg (War Sails DLC)
  Cultural inspiration: Norse/Scandinavian. King Halthdar the Golden.
  Unified a generation ago by his father Volbjorn the Hungry.
  Visual: Nord kingdom colors FF202931 / FFb7623c on armor dynamic sections,
          shields, cloth, banners, and cloaks. Round shields with geometric
          patterns. Axes everywhere. Fur cloaks. Built their own armor.
  Military: aggressive forward infantry. Shield wall that MOVES, not holds.
            Axe-throwing at close range before melee. Nords do not defend.
  Naval: THE most sea-native faction. Every unit comfortable on a ship. Their
         longships are faster and more maneuverable than Sturgian equivalents.
         Raider loot advantage is a Bannerwake-coded campaign behavior or loot
         model modifier, not an XML culture fact. Tune the number in testing;
         the old 30% figure is a starting balance target, not a locked rule.
  Lore mechanic: The Thing. Seasonal political assembly. Player can attend,
                 influence warpath priorities, observe internal Nord politics.
                 Missing it means decisions were made without you.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 4: SUB-FACTION ROSTER
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ENGINE CONSTRAINTS (confirmed by local v1.4.5 file audit):
- Minor factions are local Faction records with is_minor_faction="true"; most
  vanilla minor factions also use minor_faction_character_templates rather than
  persistent dynastic Hero rosters. The local SandBox minor factions generally
  define four leader templates, so the old "3 named hero leader cap" is not a
  confirmed engine rule.
- Ordinary minor factions are not designed as permanent landholding kingdoms.
  Settlement ownership can be changed by C# action APIs, but long-term land
  ownership requires pseudo-kingdom/full-kingdom support or equivalent custom
  handling.
- Giving a sub-faction a settlement requires C# using
  ChangeOwnerOfSettlementAction.ApplyByDefault/ApplyBySiege/ApplyByRebellion
  or another deliberately chosen action path, followed by garrison setup,
  home-settlement checks, party/AI handling, diplomacy, save-state, and
  recapture/re-emergence logic.
- Black Keel and Vrak exceed ordinary minor-faction structure deliberately by
  being pseudo-kingdom/custom-kingdom structures made of multiple clans, not a
  single ordinary minor faction.
- Faction XML requires initial_home_settlement for reliable minor-faction
  behavior. The Diathma drift warning is treated as a known modding failure
  mode caused by missing/invalid home settlement data or runtime relocation
  logic, not as a universally reproduced local proof. All new factions need
  explicit home settlement assignment in XML and C# validation on game load.
- All dynamic state transitions need custom C# campaign tracking variables

SUB-FACTION XML NAME LOCK (confirmed against local v1.4.5 + NavalDLC XML):
- Lake Rats: vanilla minor faction id `lakepike`, display/short name Lake Rats.
  Do not use `lake_rats` unless creating a new Bannerwake `bw_` record.
- Jomsvikingr: Bannerwake current display/design name for the existing
  `skolderbrotva` / Skolderbroda mercenary hook. Do not use Skolvard. Either
  migrate `skolderbrotva` deliberately or create explicit `bw_jomsvikingr`
  records; do not pretend `jomsvikingr` already exists in local XML.
- Black Keel: Bannerwake custom/pseudo-kingdom threat. No local XML minor
  faction named Black Keel exists. NavalDLC has `northern_pirates` and
  `southern_pirates`; those are source hooks, not Black Keel itself.
- Company of the Golden Boar: vanilla minor faction id `company_of_the_boar`,
  display name Company of the Golden Boar. Baldric is Bannerwake-invented.
- Grovebound: Bannerwake replacement/supersession of the vanilla `wolfskins`
  idea. No local XML clan/faction named Grovebound exists.
- Hidden Hand: vanilla minor faction id `hidden_hand`.
- Undying Flame: Bannerwake redesign/name for vanilla `embers_of_flame`.
- Last Legion: Bannerwake display/design name for vanilla
  `legion_of_the_betrayed` / Legion of the Betrayed. Local XML text already
  uses the phrase Last Legion, but the faction id/display remains the older
  one unless intentionally migrated.
- Akritoi: Bannerwake frontier-cavalry concept. No local XML minor faction
  named Akritoi/Acritoi exists. Closest XML hook is `eleftheroi`, plus the
  Elaches clan comment "Draw on Akritic ballads." Preserve or migrate this
  deliberately; do not call Akritoi XML-confirmed.
- Jawwal: vanilla minor faction id `jawwal`.
- Ghilman: vanilla minor faction id `ghilman`.
- Beni Zilal: vanilla minor faction id `beni_zilal`.
- Renegati: Bannerwake custom Imperial naval toll faction. No local XML minor
  faction named Renegati exists.
- Ironjarls: Bannerwake Nord political internal faction. No local XML minor
  faction named Ironjarls exists, and they are not mercenaries.

SUB-FACTION STATES: Every sub-faction operates in one of four states:
  DORMANT — minimal activity, barely visible
  ACTIVE  — normal behavior, regular spawning
  ESCALATED — expanded presence, event has fired
  CRISIS  — major world event, player must respond

Transition between states driven by C# campaign variables tracking time,
player inaction, military conditions, and world events. This is the most
complex technical system in Bannerwake. Build as skeleton first, test empty,
then populate with faction behaviors.

--- STURGIAN SUB-FACTIONS ---

LAKE RATS
  Culture: Sturgian (Kievan Rus cultural sphere)
  Leader: The Harbormaster (unnamed, rotates by seniority among senior wreckers)
  Region: Lake Laconis marshes and northern lake shore. NEVER leave the lake.
  Default State: Hostile to all. Wreck ships with false beacons. Rob survivors.
  Escalation: If ignored long enough, Harbormaster consolidates the beacon
    network into a formal toll operation. Any ship on Lake Laconis pays or burns.
    C# coastal-threat event fires after a player-inactivity timer.
  Units: Wrecker (melee, salvage tools as weapons), Beachcomber (light
    skirmisher), Harbormaster's Guard (heavy enforcer, elite tier)
  Threat signature: A ship you didn't know was in trouble. A beacon where no
    lighthouse should be. The wreckage in the morning.
  Notes: KEEP THE NAME EXACTLY. Keep the vibe exactly. Deepen the hierarchy
    and give them the false-beacon mechanic as real gameplay. Accepts fugitives
    from any culture who arrive — they are not ethnically Sturgian only.

STURGIAN SUB-FACTION SLOT 3 — RESERVED
  The third Sturgian slot is deliberately reserved until a concept earns it.
  Sturgia currently has two sub-factions: Lake Rats and The Jomsvikingr.
    Note: Jomsvikingr are Nord by culture but operate in Sturgian-adjacent
    northern coastal territory.
  Do not fill this slot with a forced placeholder.

THE JOMSVIKINGR
  Culture: Nord — THE Jomsviking brotherhood. Not inspired by Jomsvikings.
    They ARE the Jomsvikings. The real thing.
  Leader: Jomshersir Floki. No surname. No epithet needed. Everyone in the
    northern world knows which Floki. He has held the position for twenty years.
    Quiet, calculating, cold. Believes in the code completely AND uses it
    strategically. The most dangerous kind of man.
  Region: Northern coasts. Fixed fortified hall that predates Halthdar's
    unification. Halthdar respects them and leaves them alone. They respect
    Halthdar and ignore him.
  Default State: MERCENARY. Available to any kingdom with gold and reputation.
    The code is ABSOLUTE — no exceptions, ever, even when it destroys someone.
    They never break a contract. Ever. No violence toward women or children.
    No personal feuds within the brotherhood. All loot shared equally.
    Brothers avenged without exception.
  Escalation: If Nordvyg weakens significantly, the Jomsvikingr declare
    independence and fortify their hall as a permanent independent entity.
  THE CODE: Absolute. Not a guideline. A brother shows fear — he is expelled.
    He has been a brother for fifteen years. The code doesn't care. Floki
    enforces it without hesitation. This is what makes them terrifying and
    tragic simultaneously. They chose this. Every morning they choose it again.
    The code creates monsters and heroes simultaneously. Same oath.
  PLAYER ARC (Nord player characters ONLY — the brotherhood is Nord-exclusive):
    The deepest faction arc in Bannerwake. A Nord player can petition to join,
    climb the ranks, and become the finest warrior the brotherhood fields short
    of Floki himself. Never Jomshersir — Floki is always above. The arc runs
    from first contract to Jomsvígr across the entire campaign.
    Entry: Quest chain — Floki sets five specific tasks testing different
      aspects of the code. Fear, retreat, brotherhood, contract, and Floki's
      final question. See Section 9c for full quest and event details.
    Rank system (party size scales with rank AND skills — full detail in
      skill rework session):
      Nýr (20 troops) — just passed the trial, barely recognized
      Jomskarl (60 troops) — recognized Joms-man
      Jomsvakt (120 troops) — guards the brotherhood's interests
      Jomsthegn (200 troops) — Floki's trusted proven warrior
      Jomsstallar (260 troops) — senior officer, Floki's marshal
      Jomsvígr (300 troops) — the finest short of Floki himself
    Exclusive troops: ONLY Jomsvikingr members can recruit Jomsvikingr units.
      Recruited from the Jomsvikingr hall settlement ONLY. No other source.
      No kingdom, player, or lord can field Jomsvikingr troops without
      brotherhood membership.
  TONE REFERENCE: Vinland Saga. Events, dialogue, and encounters should feel
    like that series — the code creating monsters and heroes simultaneously,
    human reality beneath absolute rules, Floki as a calculating force of
    nature rather than a simple warrior-king.
  Threat signature: A shieldwall that holds when everything else has broken.
    The raven banner means someone paid. The raven banner also means Floki
    decided this contract was worth keeping. That distinction matters.
  Notes: Repurposes or supersedes NavalDLC Skolderbroda only after explicit
    migration audit of Faction.skolderbrotva. Raven motif is a Bannerwake
    art-direction lock supported by local NavalDLC assets such as
    `ravenfigurehead`, not an XML-confirmed Jomsvikingr faction-sigil claim.
    The Jomsvikingr's raven is disciplined and oath-bound — distinct from
    Black Keel's corrupted variant.
    Do not use Skolvard as an ID, heading, file name, or code symbol.

--- NORD SUB-FACTIONS ---

BLACK KEEL
  Culture: Nord — cursed pirate confederation
  Leader: Seajarl Hrothgar the Damned + Captain council beneath him
    (three named captains is the current design budget, not a proven engine
    limit; local minor-faction XML commonly has four leader templates)
  Region: Northern seas and Nordvyg/Sturgian coasts. PRIMARILY REGIONAL.
    Occasional parties appear near Imperial coasts — rare, alarming, never
    explained. They should not appear in southern Aserai waters.
  Default State: HOSTILE TO ALL. No mercenary state. No negotiation. Ever.
  Escalation (CRISIS): If ignored long enough, Hrothgar consolidates the captain
    council. Fires a world event. He seizes a specific coastal port using
    ChangeOwnerOfSettlementAction through a Bannerwake wrapper. Garrison is
    inflated programmatically to prevent early recapture — this is intentional,
    they should be hard to remove.
    Port becomes a Black Keel stronghold with their own culture and troop spawns.
    Retaking it requires a coordinated military campaign.
  TROOP TREE (full, confirmed design):
    Black Keel Deckhand (T1) — weak starter, barely armed, wrong eyes
      ↓                              ↓
    Black Keel Sailor (T2)         Black Keel Shipbow (T2)
    Basic crewman, shield,           Basic ship archer, simple bow, belt knife
    short sword
      ↓              ↓               ↓
    Black Keel       Black Keel     Black Keel Crow-Watcher (T3)
    Reaver (T3)      Deckstalker    Veteran ranged, better bow, good sidearm
    Core melee       (T3 branch)      ↓
    raider, axe/     Medium         Black Keel Salt-Eye (T4)
    sword+shield,    aggressive     Elite ranged pirate. Accurate, patient,
    javelins         boarder,       sea-worn. Puts the arrow exactly where
      ↓              lighter,       it should not go.
    Black Keel       offensive,
    Hullguard (T4)   two-handed
    Elite shield+    axe or 2H
    sword/axe,       weapon +
    heavier armor,   throwing
    disciplined      weapons
    Norse pirate       ↓
    at peak          Black Keel
                     Shipbreaker (T4)
                     Heavy two-handed shock.
                     Axe/maul/2H weapon.
                     Dangerous, vulnerable to missiles.
  BALANCE: Black Keel troops are a slight tier above vanilla equivalents.
    Intentional — uncanny villain faction. Not broken, but consistently wrong
    in ways that make them feel dangerous. Wrongness felt in the fight.
  ARMOR: The active local NavalDLC install does include pirate armor/materials/
    items, but the set is small and uneven. Some pieces are merely standard
    armor relabeled as pirate kit, and the overall texture/shading quality is
    inconsistent. Use NavalDLC pirate pieces only where they fit; otherwise
    reskin, reshade, and recolor existing Nord/Sturgian/mixed-source assets
    into Black Keel's dedicated palette: dark grey, tar-black, dead sea-brown,
    corroded iron, ashen cloth. No bright colors, no clean metal. Everything
    looks like it has been in saltwater. Phase 7.
  Threat signature: Three longships that were already there when you arrived.
    They were waiting. Up close, something is wrong with their eyes.
  THE WRONGNESS:
    - Skin ashen and grey, sunken eyes, pallor consistent across all tiers
    - Wounds that should drop a man do not slow them
    - Prisoners who laugh at the wrong moments
    - Source DELIBERATELY AMBIGUOUS — the sea, a pact, Hrothgar himself,
      uncharted cursed waters north of everything
    - Player pieces together fragments that never fully reconcile
    - Face retexture to grey-ashen palette is a PHASE 7 ART TASK
    - Do NOT give a definitive explanation anywhere in the mod
  Notes: Most important new sub-faction. The wrongness is the entire point.
    HERO LIMIT: Implement Black Keel as multiple clans (Hrothgar's clan + Captain
    clans) under a pseudo-kingdom/custom-kingdom structure. Do not rely on a
    companion-limit patch unless local v1.4.5 decompilation proves it is the
    correct surface.
    All clans dormant and hidden pre-escalation. Hrothgar in fog of war until
    the world event fires. Captains also hidden until escalation.

--- VLANDIAN SUB-FACTIONS ---

WOODSMEN OF ROBYNN
  Culture: Vlandian — outlaw folk heroes
  Leader: Robynn of the Wood (common-born legend, no formal title, no nobility)
    NAME ORIGIN: Deliberately echoes Robin Hood. Common-born, became a legend
    bigger than himself. Now the legend is its own cage. He cannot stop being
    Robynn of the Wood even when he wants to.
  Region: The forests of Forrath, Vlandian territory. Forrath is a PLACE NAME,
    not an organization name. They do not leave their forest.
  Default State: HOSTILE to Vlandian nobility and their convoys. NEUTRAL or
    FRIENDLY to Vlandian villages. Will not attack common travelers unprovoked.
  IMPLEMENTATION — "hostile to lords, not villages" mechanic:
    This requires a custom C# AiBehavior patch on the Woodsmen clan party logic.
    Their FindBestAction override registers ONLY the following as valid targets:
      - Lord parties (IsLord == true)
      - Noble caravans (carrying tax or luxury goods for noble households)
      - Tax collector parties
    The RaidSettlement action is explicitly SUPPRESSED for this clan always.
    Village relationship is set positive by default in XML and reinforced in C#.
    Villagers will not call for help when Woodsmen are nearby.
    Woodsmen parties will not loot villages even when low on food/gold.
    Reference: Party AI Overhaul mod for Harmony-based party behavior targeting.
  Escalation: NO CORRUPTION ARC. Robynn stays heroic throughout. He is human —
    a temper, bad decisions under pressure, men who are not all noble. Events
    fire showing the gap between legend and reality. The legend does not collapse.
    He robs noble convoys and gives to Vlandian villages. This does not change.
    There are hiccups. The heroism is real. So is the mess.
  Units: Forest Rover (light crossbow skirmisher), Woodsman (medium infantry),
    Forrath Archer (longbow specialist), Robynn's Men (elite, personally
    recruited by Robynn himself — rare, high quality)
  Threat signature: A noble's supply convoy stopped at the Forrath road. The
    next village has new grain in their storehouse that wasn't there last week.
  Notes: Supersedes Brotherhood of the Woods. Same Vlandian origin, same Robin
    Hood concept. Rebuilt with actual depth. Name is WOODSMEN OF ROBYNN
    everywhere in code and XML. Forrath remains the forest place-name only.

COMPANY OF THE GOLDEN BOAR
  Culture: Vlandian — professional mercenaries
  Leader: The Captain (named, has memory of past contracts, specific dialogue
    lines that reference previous interactions)
  Region: Calradia-wide. NOT regionally locked. They go where the contract is.
  Default State: MERCENARY. Available to any kingdom. Crossbow-specialized.
    Expensive but professional. Inspired by the Great Catalan Company.
  Escalation: Powerful and unpaid long enough, the Captain demands land as
    payment instead of gold. This is when they become genuinely dangerous.
  Units: Boar Crossbowman (core identity, multiple tiers), Boar Sergeant (heavy
    infantry), Boar Outrider (mounted crossbow), Golden Boar Veteran (elite,
    scarred and extremely dangerous)
  Threat signature: A battlefield where the enemy had crossbow superiority you
    didn't expect. Someone paid more than you did.
  Notes: THE COMPANY REMEMBERS. Betray the Golden Boar once and they will accept
    a contract against you specifically. This is a C# tracked variable per player
    interaction. Every contract kept or broken is logged permanently.
    KEEP THE NAME "Company of the Golden Boar" EXACTLY.

--- BATTANIAN SUB-FACTIONS ---

THE GROVEBOUND
  Culture: Battanian — Ca fál Old Gods zealots
  DESIGN IDENTITY: Wild, monolithic, fanatical. These are the truest Battanians
    alive — the ones who never compromised with the Empire, never built stone
    walls, never dealt. They observe the full Ca fál code not as performance but
    as genuine, maniacal belief. Terrifying to outsiders. Deeply respected and
    quietly feared even by other Battanians. They live in the deep forest.
    They paint themselves with woad. They are not a gang. They are a conviction.
  Named Heroes:
    Groveseer Brenach fen Ashyr — the spiritual leader. Eldest Groveseer.
      Has not left the sacred grove outside Dunglanys in forty years. His oath.
      When the Gathering is called it is called in his name even though he never
      moves. Player can only meet him by going to the grove themselves.
    Culwyn fen Woadmarked — the war leader. Brenach's opposite. He fights so
      Brenach does not have to. Woad-covered, two-handed weapon, deeply dangerous.
    Torcarn the Rootbound — the eldest warrior. Has observed the full Ca fál code
      longer than anyone living. No sewn clothes. No cooked food. No roof. Ever.
      Appears rarely. When he does, something is about to happen.
  Region: Battanian forests, sacred sites, the grove outside Dunglanys.
    Groveseers travel freely across ALL of Calradia by ancient law.
    Fighters stay in Battanian territory.
  Default State: HOSTILE to non-Battanians who enter their sacred areas.
    Neutral to Battanians. Groveseers are universally neutral and untouchable
    by Battanian custom. The fighters are not.
  Escalation (THE GATHERING): If a sacred grove is burned, a Groveseer is
    attacked or violated, or a significant Battanian spiritual settlement falls
    to an outsider — the Grovebound call a Gathering. Every Ca fál adherent answers. Every
    Grovebound fighter, every Battanian who still hears the old voice arrives.
    A temporary, overwhelming wave descends on the responsible party or
    settlement. Then silence. They return to the woods.
    THIS IS A CAMPAIGN EVENT, not a standing army. Gathering Warriors do NOT
    appear in normal spawn pools under any circumstances.
  Troop Tree (CONFIRMED — single line, all troops carry throwing weapons):
    Grove Wildling (T1) — barely armed, woad-daubed, full of belief.
      No armor. Fanatical charge. They don't care if they die.
      ↓
    Grove Zealot (T2) — committed, dangerous, throwing axes, woad-heavy.
      The faith has taken hold. Still light armor.
      ↓
    Grove Forestborn (T3) — the point of no return. Born to this now.
      The forest is their only world. Throwing weapons + melee.
      Mid-tier exclusive armor — non-lootable, non-merchantable.
      ↓
    Grove Oathsworn (T4) — the old gods chose them specifically.
      The most terrifying unit in the tree. Full exclusive armor.
      Non-lootable, non-merchantable, non-droppable armor.
    [Gathering Warrior — event-only, never in normal spawns]
    [Groveseer — non-combat NPC, quest-critical, travel-free]
  TROOP DOCTRINE: Fanatical melee charge. They don't stop. They don't
    retreat. Every tier carries throwing weapons — thrown before charging.
    No ranged-only units. No shield infantry. Pure aggressive faith.
  Ca fál MECHANIC: Grovebound observing the rites (no sewn clothes, no cooked
    food, no roof) cannot be legally attacked in Battanian territory. Attacking
    them costs faction-wide relationship with ALL Battanian lords. This is a
    real C# mechanic, not flavor text. Must function correctly.
  Threat signature: You attack a Battanian village. What comes out of the
    treeline is not the garrison. They were already there.
    A Groveseer appears in an Imperial city tavern without being invited. He
    sits across from you and says one thing. Then leaves.
  Notes: Supersedes vanilla Wolfskins.
    They are one people. The fighters and the seers are the same cult.
    The grove outside Dunglanys with yew-branch human sacrifice is Bannerwake
    design canon, not currently local-XML-confirmed vanilla text. Treat it as
    authored lore built to deepen Battania unless a specific vanilla string ID
    is later cited.


--- KHUZAIT SUB-FACTIONS ---

KARAKHERGIT
  Culture: Khuzait — steppe nomad holdouts
  Leader: The Free-Rider (elected by elders, title not bloodline)
  Region: Eastern steppe, Khuzait-adjacent territory. Avoid Khuzait towns.
    Specifically raid Khuzait caravans — not random targets.
  Default State: HOSTILE to Khuzait specifically. Neutral to all other factions.
    Will ally with anyone at war with the Khuzait.
  Escalation: If Khuzait lose enough territory militarily, Karakhergit declare
    those steppe regions their ancestral range and begin formal reclamation.
    If player gives them a stronghold, they reconstitute as the Khanate of Karak.
  Units: Karak Horse Archer (fast, harassment-specialist), Karak Lancer (heavier
    than typical Khuzait), Karak Elder Guard (elite, traditional steppe armor)
  Threat signature: A Khuzait caravan that arrived short. Guards say hit from
    three sides at once and attackers were gone before the dust settled.
  Notes: KEEP THE NAME "Karakhergit" EXACTLY. Their grudge against the Khan is
    ACTIVE BEHAVIOR, not flavor. They specifically target Khuzait parties and
    caravans. Inspired by clans who resisted Mongol centralization.

DUST SPEAKERS
  Culture: Khuzait — pre-Khan shamanic tradition
  Leader: No leader. They speak for the land, not for each other.
  Region: Eastern steppe and steppe borders. Travel between Khuzait clans.
  Default State: UNIVERSALLY NEUTRAL. They watch everything. They approach the
    player only through specific event triggers, not random encounters.
  Escalation: Disturb a steppe burial ground — deliberately or through ignorance
    — and the Dust Speakers become a PERMANENT, IMPLACABLE ENEMY. Their
    intelligence network closes to the player entirely. They never forgive.
  Units: Dust Speaker (non-combat advisor NPC), Burial Warden (combat-capable
    when roused, heavy ritual armor — rare but dangerous)
  Threat signature: A camp you ride through at dusk where everyone watches you
    leave and none of them speak. Three days later you know something about an
    enemy position you couldn't have known.
  Notes: Named for the dust their horses raise on the steppe — heard before
    seen, then not seen at all. Their intelligence is unmatched. They know about
    the Veil Riders, The Shroud, and the Unnamed Fleet before these events occur.
    Getting information from them requires a quest chain through steppe burial
    rites. KEEP THE NAME "Dust Speakers" EXACTLY.

--- IMPERIAL SUB-FACTIONS ---

THE HIDDEN HAND
  Culture: Imperial — criminal network
  Leader: Regional cell bosses (one per major Imperial city, no supreme leader)
  Region: Southern Imperial territory primarily. Urban and rural.
  Default State: Not openly hostile. Operate through proxies. Player encounters
    them as seemingly legitimate merchants or minor officials.
  Escalation: Player can dismantle individual cells without destroying the
    network. Each cell is semi-independent. Their relationship with the Undying
    Flame is HOSTILE — the Hand profits from instability the Flame wants to purify.
  Units: Hidden Finger (skirmisher, throwing knives, small blades), Shadow Blade
    (medium assassin), Hand Enforcer (heavy urban fighter)
  Threat signature: A quest accepted from a merchant that turns out to be a
    Hidden Hand job. Payment arrives before you deliver the goods.
  Notes: Mafia structure. Regional, not hierarchical. Each Imperial city has its
    own cell with slightly different character. Keep vanilla name.

THE UNDYING FLAME
  Culture: Imperial — fire cult
  Leader: The Vessel (elected living avatar of Darusos when cult escalates)
  Region: Imperial territory, all three factions. Spreads through settlements.
  Default State: HOSTILE to all they deem corrupt or false — which is almost
    everyone eventually. They do not negotiate. They purify.
  DOCTRINE: Overzealous, blinded by faith, genuinely sincere, genuinely
    monstrous. They believe they are just. Many innocents suffer. It is all about
    flame. They are completely sincere about the suffering they cause.
  FIRE IDENTITY: Fire at EVERY TIER, both ranged and melee.
    T1: Ember Layman — torch and rags, fire arrows
    T2-3: Flame Initiate — fire arrows, burning thrown weapons
    T4: Purifier — burning melee weapons (torches, fire-wrapped axes)
    T5 Elite: Exemplar of Saint Darusos — golden death mask if confirmed or
              authored as Bannerwake cult regalia, great weapon, burning blade
  FIRE IMPLEMENTATION: Reference Fire Arrows mod (Steam Workshop 3542272439)
    for fire arrow volley mechanics, burning damage, flaming weapon visuals.
    The fire system must work in battle — it is their entire identity. Local
    vanilla/War Sails files support fire pots, fireships, fire ballista, ship
    fire damage, and ship-burning mission events, but infantry fire arrows,
    burning thrown weapons, and burning melee weapons require custom mission
    behavior unless source-code research proves an existing implementation.
  TARGETS: They burn the corrupt AND the false. That means almost everyone.
    Imperial lords who claim the throne falsely. Villages they deem corrupt.
    Travelers who carry the wrong relics. The reeve who takes too much. All.
  Escalation: Devotion spreads through Imperial settlements over time. If
    unchecked, the cult elects a Vessel — living avatar of Emperor Darusos —
    and declares a Fourth Imperial claimant. Small, fanatical, fire everywhere.
    Reference Religions mod (Nexus 3015) for devotion spread implementation.
  Threat signature: A village that wasn't burning yesterday. Survivors say men
    in robes came from the east and deemed the reeve corrupt. The reeve was
    corrupt. This does not make what happened acceptable.
  Notes: Embers of the Flame vanilla lore establishes their Darusos/Creator
    devotion in local minor-faction text. Golden death mask and all active
    fire-combat identity must be verified by item/string ID or authored as
    Bannerwake additions. Rebuild with actual fire mechanics.

THE LAST LEGION
  Culture: Imperial — loyalist army without an Empire
  Leader: The Legate (elected by senior officers, Imperial rank structure intact)
  Region: Abandoned Imperial forts and border territories.
  Default State: Not openly hostile by default. They occupy territory and tax
    travelers through what they claim as Imperial land. Will fight if challenged.
  Escalation: Hold territory long enough and they begin administering it —
    collecting taxes, maintaining roads, acting like a functioning state. Final
    escalation: if an Empire faction reunifies enough territory, the Last Legion
    can be formally reintegrated, ending them as a minor faction but massively
    boosting that Empire faction's military strength.
  Units: Legion Footman (disciplined spear, T2-3), Legion Veteran (heavy
    infantry, T4), Praetorian (elite, full Imperial armor, T5)
  Threat signature: A fort that was abandoned six months ago now has a garrison,
    a flag, and a toll gate on the road outside it.
  Notes: Varangian Guard-to-mercenary inspiration. They want to be the army
    again. Disciplined, tragic, genuinely useful. Replaces Legion of the Betrayed.

THE AKRITOI
  Culture: Imperial — frontier cavalry mercenaries
  Leader: Archon Demetrios of the Steppe (Greek title, correct for Byzantine)
  Region: Imperial frontier zones, eastern and southern borders.
  Identity: Real Byzantine term for frontier border warriors. Elite frontier
    cavalry who have been riding the borders so long they look like the frontier
    itself. Loyal to the idea of Empire, hostile to its actual bureaucratic
    authority — they never quite submitted fully.
    Pure cavalry mercenaries. No intelligence network angle (removed).
    Fast, disciplined, expensive. The frontier is their home.
  Default State: MERCENARY. Available to Imperial-adjacent kingdoms.
    Discovery event fires within first 30 days.
  Escalation: Quest chains unlock access to their frontier knowledge — specific
    routes, passable terrain, enemy positions they've observed for years.
  Notes: Bannerwake-approved hireable mercenary concept, not a local XML-
    confirmed Akritoi faction. Local XML hook is `eleftheroi` and the Elaches
    clan comment "Draw on Akritic ballads." If implemented by migrating
    Eleftheroi, do it deliberately with compatibility notes; otherwise create
    explicit `bw_akritoi` records. Cross-regional mercenary expansion is
    reserved until explicitly approved.
    Ironjarls are NOT mercenaries — they are a Nord political internal faction.
    Do not include Ironjarls in any hireable-mercenary list.



--- ASERAI SUB-FACTIONS ---

THE JAWWAL
  Culture: Aserai — Bedouin confederacy
  Leader: The Voice (elected speaker, not military commander — title is poetic)
  Region: Nahhas desert and surrounding highlands. Claim huge swathes of desert.
  Default State: HOSTILE to caravans passing without tribute. Neutral to those
    who pay. They compose poems about their deeds and mock settled Aserai.
  Escalation: If Aserai collapses militarily, the Jawwal expand dramatically,
    claim oasis settlements, become a genuine desert power.
  Units: Jawwal Rider (camel cavalry, desert-adapted), Jawwal Skirmisher
    (javelin, light), Jawwal Poet-Warrior (elite, specific weapon traditions)
  Threat signature: A caravan that paid. Another that didn't. The difference is
    three days of travel time and knowing which.
  Notes: Poem-reciting tradition — captured Jawwal warriors give speeches rather
    than pleas. Keep name.

THE GHILMAN
  Culture: Aserai — elite slave-soldiers turned mercenary
  Leader: The Commander (appointed by senior soldiers, rotates after campaigns)
  Region: Aserai territory primarily. Available as mercenaries anywhere.
  Default State: MERCENARY. Elite, expensive, utterly loyal to contract holder.
    Mamluk-inspired. High cost, absolute loyalty while contract holds.
  THE GHILMAN RULE: They will NOT fight against another Ghilman unit regardless
    of contract. If a confrontation between Ghilman-vs-Ghilman is imminent, the
    contract flags void immediately. They are gone by morning. This rule has NO
    exceptions. This is a C# contract-monitoring behavior — when the system
    detects Ghilman-vs-Ghilman confrontation incoming, contract voids and faction
    departs. This must function correctly. Detection is custom Bannerwake logic
    over mercenary contracts, party ownership, and pending encounter targets;
    XML cannot enforce it.
  Units: Ghilman Swordsman (medium cavalry, T3), Ghilman Lancer (heavy cavalry,
    T4), Ghilman Elite (full mamluk armor, T5)
  Threat signature: A battlefield where the Ghilman on both sides stopped
    fighting each other and simply stood aside until the rest resolved itself.
  Notes: Keep name.

THE BENI ZILAL
  Culture: Aserai — traditionalist claimants
  Leader: The Elder of Zilal (keeper of the true lineage claim)
  Region: Aserai southern territory and oasis regions.
  Default State: HOSTILE to what they see as Aserai usurpers. Neutral to
    non-Aserai. They believe the current Sultan's line is illegitimate.
  Escalation: If Aserai throne instability rises (through MILITARY LOSSES, not
    any character's death), the Beni Zilal declare their claimant and become a
    political faction. They stop raiding and start campaigning.
  Units: Zilal Guard (heavy Aserai infantry), Zilal Horseman (traditional
    cavalry), Zilal Elder Guard (elite, ancient Aserai armor)
  Threat signature: A village that hasn't paid taxes to the Sultan in three
    seasons. They say their taxes go north now, to the right name.
  Notes: Escalation tied to throne instability, NOT character death. Keep name.

--- CROSS-REGIONAL SUB-FACTIONS ---

THE RENEGATI
  Culture: Imperial — renegade Imperial naval officers
  Leader: Route-Master Cassius Vorn (Latin name, bureaucratic title — they kept
    the Imperial administrative vocabulary even after abandoning the Empire)
  Region: Southern sea lanes and coastal trade routes.
  Origin: Imperial naval officers who walked away when the Empire fractured and
    kept their galleys. They didn't become pirates — they became a private toll
    authority. The bureaucracy continued, the allegiance didn't. They know every
    harbor, every chokepoint, every trade lane in the southern sea because they
    spent years defending them. Now they charge for passage instead.
  Visual: Imperial naval armor, recognizably Imperial but no longer polished.
    Rust maintained rather than removed. They look like the Empire used to look.
    Just angrier and less supervised. The eagle is still on the shield but the
    paint is old.
  Default State: Not openly hostile. They board ships, take a percentage, let
    them sail on. Calculating, businesslike. No violence unless refused.
    Early campaign discovery event fires within first 30 days.
  ESCALATION:
    ACTIVE → ESCALATED: Player-adjacent trade routes actively raided if player
      hasn't paid or established an arrangement.
    ESCALATED → CRISIS: Player attacks them consistently. Full naval harassment
      of player-adjacent trade.
  DE-ESCALATION: Player can pay tribute or establish a formal arrangement.
    Pushes back to ACTIVE. Businesslike, not personal.
  Threat signature: A ship captain who was three days late and says it was
    weather. He has a receipt he doesn't know why he kept.
  Notes: Completely distinct from Black Keel — calculating vs. cursed, organized
    vs. chaotic, southern vs. northern, businesslike vs. wrong.
    Distinct from vanilla Corsair bandit parties — Corsairs are sea looters.
    The Renegati are an organized toll operation with a paper trail.



THE IRONJARLS
  Culture: Nord — dispossessed petty kings
  Leader: Aldking Grimulf (no surname — the title carries everything)
    Aldking = ald (old in Old Norse) + king. The Old King. Pre-Halthdar
    vocabulary. A title that predates Volbjorn's unification and makes
    that clear without explanation.
    Grimulf is the most senior dispossessed jarl. His father ruled the
    largest territory before Volbjorn came with his gold and his pressure.
    He speaks for the others not because they elected him but because nobody
    disputes his seniority. That distinction matters — his authority is
    cultural, not democratic.
    He attends Halthdar's feasts when summoned. Sits at the correct place.
    Says the correct things. Has never technically broken his oath. Neither
    has any Ironjarl. They are the most dangerous kind of enemy — the ones
    who haven't moved yet.
  Region: Nord territory, inland. They hold no settlements but their parties
    patrol the territories their fathers once ruled.
  Origin: Local NavalDLC lore confirms this foundation: Halthdar's father
    Volbjorn the Hungry
    spent a decade accumulating wealth in the Empire's Vaegar Guard before
    returning
    to subjugate the petty kings. Some bent the knee. Some are still angry.
    The Ironjarls are their heirs. They remember what their fathers ruled.
    They are counting every failure Halthdar makes.
  Default State: ACTIVE from campaign start. Discovery event fires early —
    not a threat, just a presence. A name mentioned in a Nord tavern. A jarl
    who sat at the wrong end of Halthdar's feast table and didn't eat much.
  ACTIVE: Politically restless, watching. Occasionally raid Halthdar's tax
    collectors and supply convoys specifically — not villages, not common
    travelers. A political statement, not a crime. Their parties exist on
    the map as named presences.
  ESCALATED: Nordvyg loses significant territory (C# variable tracking Nord
    settlement count). Grimulf begins openly recruiting in Nord settlements.
    His parties grow. Other dispossessed jarls attend his hall.
    World notification fires: "The old names are being spoken again in
    the north."
  CRISIS: Nordvyg loses enough that the threshold is crossed. Grimulf
    declares the old kingdoms restored. Seizes a specific Nord inland
    settlement via a Bannerwake wrapper around ChangeOwnerOfSettlementAction
    — a town or castle historically part of a petty king's domain, not a
    coastal port. Garrison inflated.
    He holds it as proof that the old order still has teeth.
  DE-ESCALATION: Suppressible. If Nordvyg recovers militarily — recaptures
    territory, wins significant battles — Ironjarls return to ESCALATED then
    ACTIVE. They never disappear. They go quiet when Halthdar looks strong.
  Threat signature: A jarl who attended the feast and said nothing wrong.
    A tax collector who came back short and couldn't explain why. A name
    spoken quietly in a longhouse that the wrong person heard.
  Notes: The Ironjarls are a political internal faction, distinct from both
    Black Keel (hostile pirates) and Jomsvikingr (mercenary brotherhood).
    They fill the gap that vanilla Nords completely lack — internal political
    tension within a recently unified kingdom. They make Nordvyg feel fragile
    in a way that is historically accurate to the Norse petty king tradition.



THE SHROUD
  Culture: Pan-Calradian — advance agents of an ancient civilization
  Leader: The Reader (title, not name). The Architect who interprets
    intelligence and directs the conspiracy. Origin never revealed. May have
    been the same person for an impossibly long time. May be a title that
    passes. The fragments about The Reader are the most contradictory of all.
    The player may learn a name. It may be wrong.
  Region: Everywhere. Physical parties do not appear until late game.
  
  WHAT THE SHROUD ACTUALLY IS:
    Advance agents of an ancient civilization that predated Calradia — a
    culture that was here before Calradios the Great led his exiles northward,
    before any current kingdom existed. They were displaced, diminished, driven
    back. They have been patient. The Shroud is their instrument of return —
    agents who have spent centuries ensuring that when their masters come back,
    there is nothing left capable of stopping them.
    The Unnamed Fleet is the return. The Shroud has been preparing Calradia
    for conquest. The civil wars, the fractured kingdoms, the perpetual
    instability — none of it is accidental. All of it serves the arrangement.
    The Architects don't rule Calradia. They prepare it.
    THE SHROUD IS THE VILLAIN FACTION. Not ambiguous. Not morally complex in
    the "maybe they're right" sense. Full ideological commitment at every tier.
    Every Face chose this. Every Veil Rider serves willingly. The Reader knows
    exactly what this is and exactly why.

  RECRUITMENT: Pure recruitment — no hereditary lines. The Shroud identifies
    people with specific qualities: positioned correctly, ambitious enough,
    flexible enough in their convictions. Approached individually. Offered
    something — knowledge, power, purpose, belonging. Those who refuse tend
    to have accidents. This means a Face can be anyone. The player cannot
    profile them by bloodline. They have to notice the wrongness directly.

  THREE TIERS:
    The Faces — agents embedded as normal NPCs throughout Calradia. Lords,
      merchants, scholars, notable characters. They know everything about
      what they serve. Full ideological commitment. When exposed they don't
      plead — they look at the player with complete clarity and say nothing
      useful. They are true believers.
    The Wardens (Veil Riders) — physical military presence. Light cavalry,
      stripped of all cultural markers. No faction colors, no identifiable
      equipment. Fast, precise, elimination specialists. They don't fight
      armies. They remove specific people. When a Veil Rider party appears
      on the campaign map, someone specific is being watched or removed.
    The Architects — three to five individuals who understand everything.
      Never on the map. The player only encounters them through written
      correspondence, through agents, or through one face-to-face meeting
      deep in a quest chain. The Reader leads them.

  FRAGMENT SYSTEM (mixed fixed and dynamic):
    Fixed anchor fragments — always exist, always findable:
      - Letter on a killed Veil Rider: coordinates, a date, no signature
      - Abandoned watchtower on northern coast: always there, fresh fire-pit,
        view of nothing but sea
      - Prisoner from the Unnamed Fleet with silver sigil under collarbone
    Dynamic fragments — vary by world state and player actions:
      - Merchant in a specific city who knows too much — appears when that
        city has been in Shroud-influenced political situation
      - Dust Speaker who mentions the watchers — only with good Dust Speaker
        standing
      - Groveseer who refuses to speak about northern ruins — fires in
        certain Battanian world states
      - Lord's letter found after battle — varies by which lords player fought
    All fragments appear PASSIVELY during normal gameplay. No investigation
    mode. No fragment quest. The player accumulates understanding without
    realizing they're accumulating it.

  PLAYER CONTACT:
    Trigger: Player power threshold — when the player becomes a significant
      enough force in Calradia to matter. Not investigation — significance.
      A powerful clan, a kingdom forming, military victories that reshape
      regional politics.
    First contact: A Face appears. In a tavern, at the edge of camp, already
      in the room when the player arrives. They ask questions about decisions
      the player made that nobody should know about. They phrase it as
      curiosity. They are demonstrating omniscience, not making conversation.
      Then they offer something minor — information the player could have
      found eventually. The smallness is deliberate. They're not buying the
      player. They're showing what even their minor offerings come from.
      The player leaves with cold sweat and a small gift. No threats. No
      demands. Just demonstrated omniscience and a door left open.

  STATE TRANSITIONS:
    DORMANT → ACTIVE: Campaign start. Faces are embedded. Fragment system
      running passively. The player has no idea they exist.
    ACTIVE → ESCALATED: Player power threshold crossed. First contact fires.
      Veil Riders begin appearing on the steppe and northern coast.
    ESCALATED → CRISIS (THE SHROUD REVEALED): Enough fragments assembled AND
      Veil Riders have been encountered. The full picture begins to cohere.
      The Unnamed Fleet appears on the coastal map. This is Bannerwake's
      Pendor Order equivalent endgame layer. The player now knows what is
      coming. The game never tells them what to do about it.

  PLAYER OPTIONS (none are directed — the player chooses):
    Ignore it — the Fleet still comes. The Shroud continues arranging.
    Unite Calradia — through normal kingdom mechanics, build something capable
      of resisting the arrival.
    Dismantle The Shroud — cell by cell, Face by Face, investigation and
      targeted military action.
    Find The Reader — the deepest quest chain in Bannerwake. May be
      impossible to complete. The answer at the end may raise more questions.
    Do nothing — watch. Some players will choose this. It is valid.

  BLACK KEEL CONNECTION:
    Whatever is wrong with Hrothgar and his crew — they found it in the north.
    In deep waters. Near something that shouldn't be there. The Unnamed Fleet
    has been approaching for some time, scouting, testing, touching the world.
    Black Keel sailed into those waters. The connection is never stated.
    The player assembles it from fragments. It is never confirmed.

  DESIGN RULES (absolute, never violated):
    NEVER give a definitive answer about The Shroud anywhere in the mod.
    NEVER give a definitive answer about the Unnamed Fleet anywhere in the mod.
    NEVER give a definitive answer about The Reader's identity or origin.
    NEVER give a definitive answer about the ancient civilization.
    The ambiguity is not laziness. It is the entire point.
    The player should finish a full campaign and still not be certain of
    everything. That uncertainty is what makes The Shroud feel real.

  Threat signature: A lord who knew about your battle before your scouts
    reported it. A merchant who asks specifically about the northern coastline.
    The Unnamed Fleet on the horizon that was gone by morning. A Face who
    sits across from you and asks about a decision you made six months ago
    that you thought nobody witnessed. They knew. They have always known.




--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 5: TROOP TREE DESIGN PRINCIPLES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

UNIVERSAL RULES:
- Every unit at every tier must have a distinguishable silhouette from every
  other unit at the same tier in the same faction
- Armor choices: visual fit FIRST, stat fit second
- No unit wears equipment from another faction's color palette without lore reason
- Each faction must have at least one unit whose silhouette = immediate threat
- Elite units (T5-6) must not spawn in early-game parties
- Branch paths at T4 represent DOCTRINAL CHOICES, not stat upgrades
- Out-of-culture troops cost DOUBLE WAGE for the player clan. Implemented as
  a model/Harmony patch around DefaultPartyWageModel.GetCharacterWage and
  GetTotalWage. Encourages cultural loyalty.
- Non-lootable exclusive armor: certain high-tier units wear armor flagged
  is_merchandise="false" and/or isolated from shops/tournaments/recruit pools.
  Mission pickup/drop behavior must be verified per item type. Use
  CannotBePickedUp-style flags where valid and add custom post-battle loot
  filtering if XML flags do not fully prevent acquisition.

TIER STRUCTURE:
  T1 — Levy/Recruit:    rough cloth, basic tools. They look scared.
  T2 — Militia/Trained: first real weapon, partial armor. Beginning to look
                         like a soldier.
  T3 — Warrior:         coherent armor set. Faction colors appear clearly.
  T4 — Veteran:         full armor. Faction identity unmistakable.
  T5 — Elite:           specialized armor, high-quality weapons. Rare.
                         Immediately threatening.
  T6 — Champion/Guard:  the faction's finest. Named encounter feel.

BRANCH PHILOSOPHY:
  Each branch represents a genuine doctrinal fork, not just different weapons.
  AI lords should also reflect their branch preference in party composition.

MARINE UNITS:
  Every sea-adjacent faction has at least one marine unit type — designed to
  fight on and around ships. Marine units have:
    - Higher Athletics than equivalent land troops
    - Shorter weapons (no polearms that tangle in rigging)
    - May have thrown weapons for boarding actions
    - Available at coastal settlements and port villages, not inland
  Nords and Black Keel have marine variants at nearly every tier.
  Khuzait have ZERO marine units.

GROVEBOUND ARMOR NOTE:
  T4-T5 Grovebound units wear exclusive armor flagged non-droppable/
  non-merchantable where item flags support it, and filtered out of shops,
  tournaments, random loot, and post-battle salvage through C# if needed.
  This is intentional — their armor belongs to the old gods, not the market.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 5b: CONFIRMED FACTION TROOP TREES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

All trees confirmed through design discussion. Names are locked unless explicitly
revisited. Armor assignments are Phase 7 art tasks — ignore vanilla armor visuals.

--- STURGIA ---
Cultural identity: Pure Kievan Rus / Slavic. NO Norse elements in the main
faction. Norse elements belong to the Jomsvikingr sub-faction only.
Colors: Sturgia XML colors FF1C2A50 / FF949CCC on dynamic armor sections,
cloth, shields, and banners; iron grey, brocade, and fur trim as secondary
materials.
Military identity: Disciplined heavy Slavic infantry. Lamellar and scale armor.
Spears and axes primary. Boyar cavalry elite and rare.

STANDARD LINE:
  Spear:   Nabornik → Ratnik → Spear Voinik → Spear Starets → Spear Bogatyr
  Axe:     (from Ratnik) → Axe Voinik → Axe Starets → Axe Bogatyr
  Mariner: (from Ratnik) → Ladya Shipman → Ladya Reaver → Ladya Boarder
  Ranged:  Nabornik → Hunter → Luchnik → Elite Luchnik → Sokol
  Cavalry: Nabornik → Konnik → Veteran Konnik → Heavy Konnik → Boyar Konnik

ELITE LINE:
  Otrok → Varyag → Veteran Varyag → Gridnik (MOUNTED from here) →
  Druzhinnik → Druzhinnik Champion
  Note: Gridnik is the transition to cavalry. Druzhinnik and Champion are
  heavy cavalry. The finest mounted warriors in Sturgia.

NAMING NOTES:
  Nabornik = conscript/levy (Old Slavic)
  Ratnik = warrior/soldier (Old Slavic)
  Voinik = fighter (Old Slavic)
  Starets = elder/veteran (Old Slavic)
  Bogatyr = epic hero-warrior (Old Slavic)
  Luchnik = archer/bowman (Old Slavic)
  Sokol = falcon (Old Slavic) — the elite archer
  Konnik = horseman (Old Slavic)
  Ladya = longboat (Old Slavic) — river/lake mariner identity
  Otrok = junior household member (Old Slavic)
  Varyag = Varangian — what Slavs called Norse mercenaries absorbed into
           the druzhina. Historically correct even in a pure Slavic faction.
  Gridnik = junior druzhina cavalry member (Old Slavic)
  Druzhinnik = member of the prince's household guard (Old Slavic)


--- VLANDIA ---
Cultural identity: Norman feudal Europe. Norman-French and Germanic blend.
Colors: Vlandia XML colors FF5C2017 / FFECBA44 on dynamic armor sections,
cloth, shields, and banners; dark steel as secondary material.
Military identity: Heavy cavalry charge with crossbow suppression. Infantry
holds, cavalry decides. Every unit belongs to someone's banner.

STANDARD LINE:
  Sword/Shield: Levy → Fourtier → Man-d'Armes → Serjeant → Lion Guard
  Pike:         (from Man-d'Armes) → Piquier → Veteran Piquier
  Polearm:      (from Fourtier) → Billier → Voulgier → Halberdier
  Cavalry:      (from Man-d'Armes) → Chevaucheur → Gendarme
  Crossbow:     Levy → Arbalestier → Serjeant Arbalestier → Pavoisier →
                Cranequinier
  Mariner:      (from Serjeant Arbalestier) → Seafarer (Mariner) →
                Nautier (Mariner)

ELITE LINE:
  Squire → Gallant → Chevalier → Ritter → Banner Ritter

NAMING NOTES:
  Fourtier = invented Norman-French compound, the committed footsoldier
  Man-d'Armes = actual Norman-French term for man-at-arms
  Serjeant = historical Norman spelling of Sergeant
  Lion Guard = Vlandia's heraldic lion identity, elite infantry
  Piquier = from piquier/pike, French-adjacent
  Billier = billman, Vlandianized
  Voulgier = from vouge, real French polearm
  Halberdier = keep standard spelling
  Chevaucheur = Old French for light raiding cavalry
  Gendarme = real term, heavy mounted warrior
  Arbalestier = period French term for crossbow operator
  Pavoisier = from pavois, the large shield crossbowmen carried
  Cranequinier = from cranequin, winding mechanism on heavy crossbow
  Seafarer = Vlandian mariner, readable
  Nautier = Vlandianized from Nauta, elite mariner
  Squire = keep, entry point should be readable
  Gallant = keep
  Chevalier = actual French knight title
