  Ritter = knight in Germanic, adds the Norman-Germanic blend
  Banner Ritter = Germanic construction, the banner-bearing knight


--- BATTANIA ---
Cultural identity: Pictish, Irish, Welsh Celtic. Ca fál religion. Forest
ambush faction. Disciplined Celtic army — NOT ragtag wildmen. Woad and
nakedness belongs to the Grovebound fanatics, not the regular army.
Colors: Battania XML colors FF2D3F1D / FFBFCBB0 on dynamic armor sections,
cloth, shields, and banners; grey linen and dark iron as secondary materials.
Gold Celtic knotwork as mark of status. Tartan/plaid at lower tiers.
Chainmail and scale from mid-tier upward. Round shields with Celtic spirals.
Fur mantles at elite tier. Conical helmets with nasal guards.
Military identity: Ambush and terrain exploitation. Lethal in forests.
All troops carry throwing weapons — javelins or throwing axes.

STANDARD LINE:
  Melee/Shield: Clanborn → Clanward → Fernrath → Fernrath Proven →
                Tartan Guard
  Falx:         (from Fernrath) → Falxarn → Gwyl Falxarn
  Cavalry:      (from Fernrath) → Scout → Greenrider
  Mariner:      Clanborn → Riverborn → Fernrower → Greenfarer (Mariner) →
                Morguard (Mariner)

ELITE LINE (T2 through T6, starts at higher power baseline):
  Highborn → Gwylwatcher → Gwylwarden → Fian → Fian Champion

NAMING NOTES:
  Clanborn = born into the clan, unproven
  Clanward = clan's ward/committed fighter
  Fernrath = fern (forest plant) + rath (committed), the proven infantry
  Fernrath Proven = veteran of the Fernrath identity
  Tartan Guard = elite shield infantry, tartan is visually specific to
                 Battanian Celtic identity from the wallpapers
  Falxarn = falx + arn (warrior suffix), the two-handed falx bearer
  Gwyl Falxarn = gwyl (dark/fierce) + Falxarn, the elite falx warrior
  Scout = light cavalry, readable
  Greenrider = elite cavalry, green is their color
  Riverborn = born on the river, mariner identity
  Fernrower = forest warrior who rows, T3 mariner
  Greenfarer = green + farer, the coastal raider, T4 mariner
  Morguard = mor (sea Welsh) + guard, elite sea guardian, T5 mariner
  Highborn = noble youth, entry to elite line
  Gwylwatcher = gwyl (dark/fierce) + watcher, the noble in deep training
  Gwylwarden = gwyl + warden, the almost-Fian, one step from the title
  Fian = canonical Celtic warrior-poet tradition, earned title
  Fian Champion = the pinnacle. Do not touch. Ever.

VISUAL REFERENCE: War Sails wallpapers show Battania as a disciplined Celtic
  army — proper conical helmets, chainmail, Celtic knotwork shields in dark
  teal/green with gold spirals, fur mantles, tartan lower garments. The woad-
  painted naked warrior is the EXCEPTION at T1-T2 and the Grovebound only.
  From T3 upward, Battanian troops look like Image 4 from the wallpaper set:
  armored, proud, culturally distinct.


--- NORDS ---
Cultural identity: Norse/Scandinavian. King Halthdar the Golden.
  Unified a generation ago by Volbjorn the Hungry. Sea-native faction.
Colors: Nord kingdom colors FF202931 / FFb7623c. Armor dynamic sections,
  shields, cloth, banners, and cloaks should resolve to those colors by default.
  Round shields with geometric patterns. Axes everywhere. Fur cloaks. Built
  their own armor.
Military identity: Pure infantry — NO cavalry. The sea is their mobility,
  their feet are their battlefield presence. Every Nord unit is a mariner
  by default — NO separate mariner line needed. All units fight on ships
  with full effectiveness.
Doctrine: Aggressive forward infantry. Shield wall that MOVES, not holds.
  Axe-throwing at close range before melee. Nords do not defend — they press.

STANDARD LINE:
  Shield/Spear: Youngling → Drengr → Fyrdkarl → Thegn → Huskarl
    Note: Shield/spear line carries spear + one-handed weapon + shield.
    Not pure spearmen — versatile shield infantry. Spear is primary.
    Huskarl = the Jarl's finest household warrior. Iconic Nord elite.

  Axe:          Youngling → Drengr → Shieldkarl → Axdrengr → Skjaldbrestir
    Note: Shieldkarl carries shield + one-handed axe. Axdrengr drops the
    shield and goes two-handed. Skjaldbrestir = shield-breaker, real Old
    Norse. The peak of aggressive Nord axe warfare.

  Archer:       Youngling → Huntsmann → Bowmann → Eldmann → Sky-Gods Chosen
    Note: Every tier carries a melee weapon as backup — Nord archers are
    not passive. Sky-Gods Chosen are blessed by the high gods in Nord
    religious tradition. Their arrows are ritually prepared. Not magic —
    myth. The player feels the difference.

ELITE LINE (T3 through T6, splits at T5):
  Note: The elite line IS the berserker tradition. Common infantry are
  disciplined shield warriors. The elite are those who gave themselves
  to fury. The animal transformation is the story of this line.

  Wildling (T3) — entered the berserker tradition's outer circle
  Warfang (T4) — the fury is building, fights harder than he should
    ↓                              ↓
  Bjornfang (T5)              Ulffang (T5)
  Bear-warrior emerging        Wolf-warrior emerging
    ↓                              ↓
  Berserkir (T6)              Ulfhedinn (T6)
  Bear-warrior, pure rage      Wolf-warrior, predatory
  Heavy, unstoppable           Fast, gets inside formations
  No shield, two-handed        Atgeir wielder, wolf cloak

NAMING NOTES:
  Youngling = young Nord, entry level, readable
  Drengr = brave man/warrior, real Old Norse, shared T2 for shield and axe
  Fyrdkarl = fyrd (levy/muster) + karl (man), committed levy warrior
  Thegn = real Old Norse rank, proven warrior in Jarl's service
  Huskarl = real Old Norse, the Jarl's household guard, iconic
  Shieldkarl = shield + karl, the shielded axeman before specialization
  Axdrengr = axe + drengr, the proven two-handed axeman
  Skjaldbrestir = shield-breaker, real Old Norse compound
  Huntsmann = huntsman with Nordic -mann suffix
  Bowmann = bowman with Nordic -mann suffix
  Eldmann = eld (old/fire Norse) + mann, the veteran archer
  Sky-Gods Chosen = blessed by the Norse high gods, ritually prepared
  Wildling = entered the berserker outer circle, animal beginning
  Warfang = war + fang, the fang of war, animal fully emerging
  Bjornfang = bjorn (bear Norse) + fang, the bear-fang warrior
  Ulffang = ulf (wolf Norse) + fang, the wolf-fang warrior
  Berserkir = bear-warrior, real Old Norse, the consumed fury
  Ulfhedinn = wolf-warrior, real Old Norse, wolf cloak tradition


--- ASERAI ---
Cultural identity: Arab caliphate and Bedouin. Sultan Unqid. Desert military
  sultanate. The Mameluke tradition is the spine. The Faris cavalry is the
  hammer. Disciplined, proud, layered.
Colors: Aserai XML colors FF965228 / FF4F2212 on dynamic armor sections,
cloth, shields, and banners; white linen and brass metal as secondary
materials.
  Calligraphic Arabic on shields. Turbaned conical helmets throughout.
  Lamellar over padded robes from mid-tier upward. Round gold shields.
Military identity: Desert sultanate army. More heavily armored than vanilla
  suggests — the wallpaper shows proper lamellar at T3-T4. Every unit carries
  multiple weapon types: polearm + blade + shield + throwing weapon.
  Mobile and versatile. Cavalry is the decisive arm.

STANDARD LINE (all branch from Aserai Tribesman T1):
  Infantry: Tribesman → Sayfiyya (T2) → Warrior (T3) → Veteran Warrior (T4)
            → Mubariz (T5)
    Sayfiyya = sword-bearer (Arabic)
    Mubariz = champion/duelist (Arabic), real historical term

  Archer:   Tribesman → Rammi (T2) → Archer (T3) → Master Archer (T4)
            → Bahriyyah Archer (T5, Mariner)
    Rammi = thrower/shooter (Arabic)
    Master Archer = keep, earned and iconic
    Bahriyyah Archer = naval archer, the archer who fights at sea

  Mariner:  Tribesman → Mallah (T2) → Bahriyya (T3, Mariner)
            → Bahriyyah (T4, Mariner) → Bahriyyah Mahir (T5, Mariner)
    Mallah = sailor (Arabic)
    Bahriyya/Bahriyyah = naval/maritime (Arabic), already in vanilla
    Mahir = skilled/expert (Arabic)

MAMELUKE LINE (separate recruitment — sultanate slave-soldier tradition):
  Mamluk Jundi (T2) → Mamluk Askari (T3) → Mamluk Haras (T4)
  → Mamluk Guard (T5) → Mamluk Palace Guard (T6)
    Jundi = soldier (Arabic)
    Askari = military man (Arabic)
    Haras = guardian (Arabic)
    Palace Guard = keep, iconic Aserai elite identity

ELITE CAVALRY LINE:
  Youth (T2) → Rakib (T3) → Faris (T4) → Veteran Faris (T5)
  → Vanguard Faris (T6)
    Youth = keep, readable entry point
    Rakib = rider (Arabic)
    Faris = horseman/knight (Arabic), canonical, keep
    Veteran Faris = keep
    Vanguard Faris = keep, the finest Aserai cavalry, iconic

VISUAL REFERENCE: Aserai wallpaper shows naval landing scene — turbaned
  conical helmets, deep red cloaks, gold calligraphic shields, lamellar
  armor, dhow-style ships. The central figure (Faris/senior Mameluke) wears
  full lamellar with white-plumed turbaned helmet and red cloak. Flanking
  soldiers at mid-tier have proper conical turbaned helmets and round gold
  shields. NOT light desert skirmishers — a proud sultanate military.


--- KHUZAIT ---
Cultural identity: Mongol and central Asian steppe. Monchug Khan.
  The Khan's unification is recent and brittle. On a ship, Khuzait troops
  look wrong. That is intentional — they belong on horses.
Colors: Khuzait XML colors FF468C7C / FFCCBB89 on dynamic armor sections,
cloth, shields, and banners; grey lamellar and leather as secondary materials.
  Deliberately uniform — the Khan's army looks like an army.
  Small teal round shields with crescent motif. Quivers on back.
Military identity: Horselords first. Infantry exists for sieges and
  defensive engagements but cavalry is the identity. Khuzait infantry
  are cavalry warriors who dismounted — heavier than their horse
  counterparts but carrying the same steppe warrior aesthetic.
Naval: NONE. Zero mariner units. The Khuzait cannot threaten by sea.
  This must be felt by the player as a strategic fact.

STANDARD LINE (all from Khuzait Arat T1 — arat = common nomad herdsman):

  Infantry line — dismounted steppe warriors:
  Arat → Nokor (T2) — companion warrior, Mongolian
       → Zurag (T3) — fighter, invented Mongolian-adjacent
       → Darkhan (T4) — privileged warrior, real Mongolian title, keep
       → Darkhan Baatar (T5) — hero-Darkhan, finest foot warrior
    Nokor = real Mongolian companion-warrior term
    Zurag = invented, Mongolian-adjacent, readable
    Darkhan = real Mongolian title, already in vanilla
    Baatar = hero/warrior in Mongolian (as in Ulaanbaatar)

  Horse Archer line — the Khuzait identity:
  Arat → Bowrider (T2) — readable, role-clear
       → Mergen (T3) — skilled archer, real Mongolian term
       → Horse Archer (T4) — keep, iconic
       → Heavy Horse Archer (T5) — keep, iconic Khuzait unit

  Heavy Cavalry line — the hammer of the steppe:
  Arat → Rider (T2) — simple, readable
       → Lancer (T3) — keep, clear role
       → Heavy Lancer (T4) — keep
       → Baatar (T5) — hero-warrior, elite heavy cavalry peak

ELITE LINE (all vanilla names kept — correct and strong):
  Noble's Son → Qanqli → Torguud → Kheshig → Khan's Guard
  These are all real Mongolian/Turkic military terms. Do not change them.

NAMING NOTES:
  Arat = common nomad herdsman, real Mongolian. Replaces Peasant/Nomad.
  Nokor = companion/warrior, real Mongolian
  Mergen = skilled archer, real Mongolian — perfect for T3 horse archer
  Baatar = hero/warrior title, Mongolian
  Darkhan Baatar = compound title, the honored hero-warrior of the steppe
  Qanqli, Torguud, Kheshig, Khan's Guard = all canonical, all correct


--- EMPIRE (all three factions share one tree) ---
Cultural identity: Late Roman / early Byzantine. Three claimants, one
  legacy. Bureaucratic, disciplined, layered. Byzantine aesthetic —
  NOT pure Roman. Constantinople, not Rome.
Colors: Imperial XML color/color2 by owning faction: Northern FF39223F /
FFDE9953, Southern FF9382D0 / FFDE9953, Western FF9E5072 / FFDE9953. Dynamic
armor sections, cloth, shields, and banners must resolve to owner colors;
silver scale and gold eagle remain shared Imperial material language.
  Western: dark burgundy-purple (#591645)
  Northern: slate and gold (#793191)
  Southern: lighter purple, more ornate (#382188)
Military identity: Formation warfare. Infantry holds. Archers punish.
  Cavalry exploits gaps. The Empire wins by not making mistakes. The
  most disciplined and versatile faction in Calradia.

STANDARD LINE (all from Imperial Recruit T1):

  Infantry line:
  Recruit → Imperial Milites (T2) — miles pluralized, readable Latin
          → Imperial Skutatos (T3) — shield infantry, Byzantine Greek
          → Imperial Scutarius (T4) — veteran shield bearer, Latin-Byzantine
          → Imperial Legionary (T5) — keep, iconic

  Crossbow/Archer line:
  Recruit → Imperial Toxotes (T2) — archer, Byzantine Greek, short
          → Imperial Crossbowman (T3) — readable, role-clear
          → Imperial Sergeant Crossbowman (T4) — keep
          → Imperial Palatine Guard (T5) — keep, iconic

  Polearm line:
  Recruit → Imperial Milites (T2, shared with infantry)
          → Imperial Kontaratoi (T3) — spear/polearm bearer, Byzantine Greek
          → Imperial Menavliaton (T4) — keep, Byzantine, iconic
          → Imperial Elite Menavliaton (T5) — keep

  Mariner line:
  Recruit → Imperial Nautes (T2) — sailor, Byzantine Greek
          → Imperial Naute (T3, Mariner) — keep, already in vanilla
          → Imperial Droungarios (T4, Mariner) — naval officer, real Byzantine
          → Imperial Katepano (T5, Mariner) — commander, real Byzantine title

ELITE CAVALRY LINE (T2-T6 only, the highest ceiling in the Empire):
  Imperial Vigla → Imperial Equite → Imperial Tagmata → Imperial Cataphract
  → Imperial Elite Cataphract
    Vigla = Byzantine palace guard unit, real term
    Equite = horseman, Latin
    Tagmata = elite Byzantine professional army unit, real term
    Cataphract = heavily armored cavalry, Byzantine/Sassanid term, iconic
    Elite Cataphract = the gold standard for shock cavalry in Calradia

NAMING NOTES:
  Milites = common soldiers, Latin plural of miles
  Skutatos = shield infantry, Byzantine Greek — Skutatoi were the backbone
             of the Byzantine army
  Scutarius = shield bearer, Latin-Byzantine blend
  Toxotes = archer in Byzantine Greek
  Kontaratoi = spear bearers, Byzantine Greek
  Menavliaton = polearm fighter, Byzantine — already in vanilla, keep
  Nautes = sailors, Byzantine Greek
  Droungarios = Byzantine naval officer title, real historical rank
  Katepano = Byzantine military governor/commander, real historical title
  Tagmata = the professional standing army of the Byzantine Empire,
            real historical institution

VISUAL REFERENCE: Empire wallpaper shows naval landing — purple-plumed
  crested helmet on the central officer figure, scale armor over chainmail,
  purple cloak, oval purple shield with gold eagle. Flanking soldiers have
  conical helmets, purple oval shields with gold eagle, spears. Massed
  uniform formations in background. This is a Byzantine army at its finest —
  posh, flashy, ornate, professional. The purple is the identity.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 6: NAVAL SYSTEM
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The sea is not scenery. Every faction wallpaper in the reference package is set
on or near water. This was not an accident.

THE SEA AS A STRATEGIC LAYER:
- Coastal settlements permanently under threat (scales with Black Keel,
  Sturgian, Renegati activity). Local NavalDLC settlements include port
  coordinates, port scenes, navalraid village scenes, and shipyard buildings.
- Ports are economic multipliers through Bannerwake code. Local XML provides
  port/shipyard data; port income and trade-route health must be computed in
  settlement economy/campaign behavior code.
- Blockading a port is a legitimate war strategy. Local API/audit evidence
  includes blockade activation/deactivation events and PortStateHelper
  restricted/trade states; Bannerwake must connect those to war, trade, patrol,
  and settlement-economy consequences.
- Storm seasons reduce naval activity through weather/ship-damage model work.
  Use DefaultMapWeatherModel, DefaultCampaignShipDamageModel, wind data, and
  ship seaworthiness; do not assume XML alone changes faction movement.
- Nords and Black Keel operate in winter seas that ground Vlandian galleys as
  an authored faction/weather asymmetry. Implement by ship class, seaworthiness,
  party AI, and storm-damage tuning.
- KHUZAIT CANNOT THREATEN BY SEA. This must be felt by the player.

PORT SETTLEMENT MECHANICS:
- Port income: generated by custom settlement-economy behavior based on trade
  route health, local security, blockades, pirate pressure, shipyard level, and
  faction naval control.
- Shipyard building: local NavalDLC settlements already include
  building_shipyard entries. Use or extend those to unlock marine recruitment,
  ship construction/repair/upgrades, and fleet-related quests.
- Coastal fortification: reduces raid damage and adds naval patrol spawns
  through C# tied to settlement buildings and DefaultSettlementPatrolModel.
- Harbormasters: new notable type who gives naval quests and intelligence.
  This requires new notable definitions/dialogue/settlement placement, not just
  a building entry.
- Pirate tribute: some minor settlements pay Renegati or Black Keel —
  player can discover and intervene, or take the arrangement for themselves

THE UNNAMED FLEET (late-game event):
  A fleet with no banner appears on the coastal map. It does not raid immediately.
  It watches. Investigation across multiple quest chains reveals fragments: old
  coins, a language no one speaks, a fighting style no one teaches. Connected to
  The Shroud. When it strikes, it is coordinated, overwhelming, unlike anything
  encountered before. This is Bannerwake's Pendor event equivalent.
  Never give a complete answer about what the Unnamed Fleet is.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 7: LORE-DRIVEN CAMPAIGN MECHANICS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

MAJOR FACTION EVENTS (these are world behaviors, not random rolls):
  Boyar Council (Sturgia): fires on significant military losses. Surviving
    boyars assess Grand Prince Raganvad. Outcomes: unified warpath, internal
    tension, or diplomatic fracture the player can influence.

  Feudal Obligation Crisis (Vlandia): fires when Derthert loses a major siege.
    Low-loyalty lords may seek independence.

  Imperial Legitimacy (Empire): fires when one faction controls 60%+ of former
    Imperial territory. Cross-faction recognition or contest event.
    Player vote carries weight if eligible.

  Grove Council (Battania): before Battania can declare war on a major faction,
    the Grove Council convenes. Slow to decide, unbreakable once committed.

  The Thing (Nords): seasonal assembly. Player can attend, present cases,
    observe internal Nord politics. Missing it = world decided without you.

  Caravan Disruption (Aserai): systematic caravan destruction weakens Aserai
    armies within ~60 in-game days. Tracked by C# variable.

LATE-GAME THREAT EVENTS:
  The Reaver Tide: Black Keel ignored long enough. Hrothgar consolidates.
    Seizes a port. Northern coasts enter Crisis state.

  The Vessel: Undying Flame spreads unchecked. They elect a Vessel of Darusos.
    Declare Fourth Empire. Fire everywhere.

  The Gathering: A sacred Battanian site is desecrated. Grovebound summon
    every Ca fál adherent simultaneously. Temporary, overwhelming.

  The Shroud Revealed: Enough fragments assembled. Shroud's military arm
    becomes visible. Unnamed Fleet appears. Endgame threat layer activates.

  THE BANDIT KING EVENT — Vrak the Foul:
    Trigger conditions (all must be true, tracked by C# campaign variables):
      - Bandit party count in Vlandian territory exceeds a threshold
      - No major faction or player has cleared bandit parties in X days
      - At least three distinct bandit types active simultaneously in region
    World notification: "Word comes from the Vlandian forests. Someone has
      given the wolves a name to follow."
    Vrak the Foul emerges — common-born, brutal, twenty years building a
      network of looter bands. Vlandian borderlands origin.
    His army: majority cheap looter-tier troops, but with a hardened veteran
      core that has been with him for years. A mob with a dangerous center.
    Settlement seizure: Vrak seizes a specific Vlandian border castle via a
      Bannerwake wrapper around ChangeOwnerOfSettlementAction. Garrison set
      high programmatically.
    AI patch: his clan's AI is patched to refuse abandoning the castle under
      normal NPC raiding logic. Lords will not casually siege it. It requires
      the player or a major faction to actively campaign against it.
    Behavior: not political. Does not want a kingdom. Uses the castle as a
      base of operations. If ignored, begins systematically raiding surrounding
      Vlandian villages and cutting road trade.
    Resolution: Defeating, routing, or driving out Vrak scatters his army back
      into normal bandit parties within days. Vrak himself does not die; the
      campaign assumes NO DEATH and his re-emergence logic remains available.
      The castle returns to normal ownership logic.
    He is friendly to looters and has a looter/bandit-based army — this is
      intentional. He gives the scattered wolves a name, not a purpose.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 7b: LEADER TITLES AND NAMED HEROES (FULL ROSTER)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

NAMING CONVENTIONS BY CULTURE (inferred from local XML names and vanilla style):
  Battanian: Welsh/Brythonic Celtic. Short, consonant-heavy. fen [name] suffix
    for clan. Examples: Caladog, Aradwyr, Melidir, Culharn, Wythuin, Brighan.
    NO English compound words. No "Ashfoot", "Ironvow", "Deathwhisper" as names.
    Those can be epithet/titles only if absolutely needed.
  Sturgian: Kievan Rus / Slavic-Norse blend. Examples: Raganvad, Gundaroving,
    Drevko. Slavic consonant clusters, occasional Norse elements at elite tier.
  Nord: Old Norse. Examples: Halthdar, Volbjorn, Halvdan, Hrothgar, Járneiðr.
  Vlandian: Norman/Frankish. Examples: Derthert, Aldric, Folcun, Cortain, Meroc.
  Imperial: Byzantine Greek / Latin. Examples: Lucon, Garios, Rhagaea, Quintus.
  Aserai: Arabic. Ibn (son of), Abu (father of), al- (the). Examples: Unqid.
  Khuzait: Mongolian. Examples: Monchug, Jebe, Sukhbat, Altun, Bayar.

CONFIRMED LEADER NAMES AND TITLES:

  Lake Rats:
    Title: The Harbormaster
    Name: Drevko (no surname — Sturgian underclass, Slavic phonetics)

  Jomsvikingr:
    Title: Jomshersir
    Name: Floki (no surname, no epithet needed. Everyone knows which Floki.)

  Black Keel:
    Title: Seajarl
    Name: Hrothgar the Damned (Old Norse. The Damned is what captains call him.)

  Woodsmen of Robynn:
    Title: none (he refuses one)
    Name: Robynn of the Wood (deliberately echoes Robin Hood — English here
      by design since Vlandia = Norman/English cultural blend)

  Company of the Golden Boar:
    Title: Captain-General
    Name: Baldric the Boar (Baldric = Norman/Germanic-adjacent, Vlandian
      phonetic fit, no collision with vanilla Aldric dey Tihr)

  Grovebound:
    Title: Groveseer
    Name: Brenach fen Ashyr (Brenach = Bannerwake-invented Brythonic/
      Battanian phonetic construction. Do NOT use Branoc; Branoc is an active
      vanilla fen Morcar noble. fen Ashyr = of the dark old, invented Celtic
      root following correct phonetic pattern)
    War leader: Culwyn fen Woadmarked (Culwyn = Celtic, correct)
    Elder: Torcarn the Rootbound (Torcarn = Bannerwake-invented Brythonic/
      Battanian phonetic construction; Rootbound = title/epithet, not a
      surname. Do NOT use Taorse here; Taorse is an active fen Eingal noble.)

  Karakhergit:
    Title: Free-Rider
    Name: Jebe Kharkhuu (Jebe = real Mongol name. Kharkhuu = harsh/strict
      in Mongolian. Correct.)

  Dust Speakers:
    Title: Bone-Reader
    Name: Sukhbat the Still (Sukhbat = real Mongolian name. Correct.)

  Hidden Hand:
    Title: The Finger (regional cell title, not supreme leader)
    Name: Verenikos (Byzantine Greek, correct. No surname given deliberately —
      he has one. The Hand doesn't use it.)

  Undying Flame:
    Title: The Vessel (only after escalation)
    Name: Theovros the Burning (Greek-adjacent, fits Imperial culture. Correct.)

  Last Legion:
    Title: Legate
    Name: Quintus Pallaros (Latin first name, Latin-ish surname. Correct.)

  Akritoi:
    Title: Archon
    Name: Demetrios of the Steppe (Greek, Byzantine. Correct.)

  Jawwal:
    Title: The Voice
    Name: Abu Raml al-Sarab (Abu = father of, Raml = sand, al-Sarab = the
      mirage. Correct Arabic naming structure. Poetic and fitting.)

  Ghilman:
    Title: Commander
    Name: Altun Bayar (Altun = gold in Turkic/Mongol. Bayar = joy. Mamluk
      soldiers had Turkic names. Correct.)

  Beni Zilal:
    Title: Elder of Zilal
    Name: Rahim ibn Zilal (ibn = son of. Arabic patronymic. Correct.)

  Renegati:
    Title: Route-Master
    Name: Cassius Vorn (Cassius = Latin/Imperial. Vorn = invented. Imperial
      renegade naval officer. Acceptable.)

  Ironjarls:
    Title: Aldking
    Name: Grimulf (no surname — the title carries everything)

  The Shroud:
    Title: The Reader (the Architect who interprets intelligence. Never named.)
    Name: Unknown. Never revealed directly.

  Vrak the Foul (Bandit King event, not a sub-faction):
    Title: none (he never took one)
    Name: Vrak the Foul (Vrak = invented, Vlandian-adjacent phonetics. Correct.)


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 8: MOD REFERENCE LIBRARY — FEATURE ANALYSIS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Every mod here is a research or private-use reference, not proof and not a
runtime dependency unless explicitly promoted later. Descriptions, workshop
pages, Nexus pages, and changelogs are hostile evidence: useful leads, often
wrong in detail. Before copying an approach, Claude Code must read the source
or inspect the installed files against local beta v1.4.5 + NavalDLC v1.2.5.
Bannerwake studies useful implementation patterns and then builds integrated
versions that serve the world rather than bolt-on additions.

--- ASSET SOURCES (private use) ---

NEXUS 2829 — Open Source Armory (OSA)
  1,600+ armors, 83 shields, 148 weapons. All lore-friendly. Does NOT
  auto-assign to troops — we control exactly which units wear which armor.
  PRIMARY armor asset source for the visual pass.

NEXUS 2349 — Swadian Armoury
  Vlandian-focused. 37+ helmet variations, brigandines, plate. Primary asset
  source for Vlandia T4-T6: Cranequinier, Banner Ritter, Lion Guard armor.
  Corrected research note: Nexus 1778 is Hallow Plate Armor, not Swadian
  Armoury. Swadian Armoury's correct Nexus page is 2349.

NEXUS 8764 — Terra Armarium
  48 historically accurate medieval weapons/shields. Weapon variety for
  Vlandian and Empire lines. Vouge for Voulgier. Heavy crossbow for Cranequinier.

--- FIRE SYSTEM (Undying Flame) ---

NEXUS 4547 — Fire Swords Polearms and Axes (confirmed by user)
  Burning/flaming visual effects on melee weapons. Fire-wrapped axes, burning
  sword visuals, flaming polearms.
  Bannerwake: Combined with Steam 3542272439 = complete Undying Flame fire
  identity. We build ONE unified fire system covering both weapon types rather
  than two separate mods. Fire is Undying Flame exclusive — no other faction
  uses it. Every tier from torch-and-rags T1 to golden-death-mask T5 has fire.

STEAM 3542272439 — Fire Arrows
  Arrow volley AI commands, fire damage, burning visual, deployment buildings,
  crafting fuel/wick, burning damage tick.
  Bannerwake: Fire arrow volley mechanics for Undying Flame ranged tiers. The
  AI volley command is critical — Flame Initiates fire VOLLEYS, not individual
  arrows. Bannerwake version integrates both fire types in one system.

--- SIEGE SYSTEM ---

NEXUS 8958 — Siege Engines Extended
  New siege engine types, rebalanced HP/targeting, improved siege AI,
  barricades, doubled equipment variety. War Sails compatible.
  Bannerwake: Faction-differentiated siege capability. Empire = best siege
  variety (Last Legion identity). Nords = worst (they board, not engineer).
  Black Keel port seizure event needs varied siege equipment. Undying Flame
  gets burning siege equipment variants.

--- ENCOUNTER AND ROLEPLAYING SYSTEMS ---

STEAM 3172213744 — Adventurer
  Bounty notices in town taverns, heroes approaching player in towns for
  chat/duels/gifts, street encounters, caravan escort missions. You noted you
  don't like it as-is, but its systems are relevant.
  Bannerwake improvement: Build our own lore-aware encounter system. Every
  encounter is scripted and faction-specific, not random. A Shroud Face sits
  across from you in an Imperial city tavern. A Groveseer asks one question.
  A Hidden Hand agent offers a "merchant contract." A tavern keeper posts a
  bounty on Black Keel raiders. Each encounter reflects current world state.

--- NOBLE TITLES AND IDENTITY ---

NEXUS 9794 — Titles
  Accurate noble titles per lord based on fiefs and status. Cultural/gender
  variants. Universal rank icons. War Sails compatible.
  Bannerwake: Sub-faction leader titles displaying correctly in dialogue and
  on the map. Seajarl Hrothgar. Groveseer Brenach fen Ashyr. The Voice.
  We build a lightweight version specific to Bannerwake's sub-faction leaders.
  The culture-specific title assignment in XML is the reference architecture.

--- LEGENDARY ITEMS AND RELICS ---

NEXUS 8777 — Champion's Relics (Tournaments Enhanced)
  16-39 relic items as tournament prizes. NPCs win and equip culture-
  appropriate relics. Onlookers gain combat XP. Lords equip culturally
  appropriate items with better stats.
  Bannerwake: Our legendary items are NOT random tournament drops. They are
  specific items tied to specific lore events — a relic from the Unnamed Fleet,
  a weapon carried by a Shroud Warden, an item from the Grovebound's grove.
  Each has a story and specific acquisition path. The NPC culture-appropriate
  equipment system we implement wholesale — this is essential for visual
  identity and is a Phase 7 requirement.

--- FACTION BEHAVIOR ---

STEAM 3676690880 — Horde Kingdom
  Proclaim nomadic kingdom without a fief. SACK towns after sieging — steal
  wealth and deal damage without claiming. Create kingdom via companion.
  Bannerwake: The SACK mechanic applies to Black Keel, Sturgian, and Nord
  raiding behavior. When a raiding party hits a coastal settlement, they sack
  it — taking wealth and dealing prosperity damage without claiming ownership.
  We build this into raider faction AI specifically, not as a player option.
  Black Keel parties auto-sack when they reach a settlement. Sacking generates
  a dramatic world notification. Evidence is left behind.

--- WEATHER AND ATMOSPHERE ---

STEAM 3626222686 — Immersive Storms
  Storms extended beyond sea to land. Doubled storm count. Storms lower morale
  and cavalry speed. Small injury chance. Dramatic visual during storms.
  Bannerwake: Integrates with the naval layer and "sea is dangerous" pillar.
  Storm seasons affect naval activity through Bannerwake weather/ship-damage
  model work. Storms affect cavalry factions (Khuzait) harder than infantry
  (Nords) only if mission/campaign speed and morale penalties are implemented.
  Bannerwake version: Nords/Black Keel operate in winter storms that ground
  Vlandian galleys through ship class, seaworthiness, and AI restrictions.
  Region-appropriate weather: north = winter storms, south = sandstorms,
  steppe = dust storms that slow horses but not infantry.

--- SKILL AND PROGRESSION ---

NEXUS 9372 — Esoteric Knowledge
  Merges Lifelong Learning + Osmosis Education + College of Calradia.
  Guaranteed minimum learning rate, passive XP from companions in roles.
  Bannerwake: "Earned progression" philosophy. Elite troops should be hard
  to get and feel valuable. We don't copy directly but the philosophy
  informs Bannerwake's progression design throughout.

--- AI AND NPC BEHAVIOR ---

NEXUS 9711 — AI Influence (AI Diplomacy)
  AI-generated NPC dialogue, AI-driven diplomacy, dynamic world events,
  NPC memory system.
  Bannerwake: We don't use AI-generated dialogue (too unpredictable for a
  lore-specific mod). But the reaction event hook architecture is the reference.
  We build scripted, lore-specific NPC reactions to world events. When the
  Undying Flame burns a village, nearby lords comment. When Black Keel
  escalates, coastal notables express fear. Scripted, not AI-generated.

STEAM 2875905594 — Special Troops Plus
  Culture-specific elite units outside normal recruitment pool.
  Bannerwake: Implementation model for exclusive non-lootable high-tier units.
  Grovebound Oathsworn, Druzhinnik Champion, Fian Champion — non-lootable armor,
  spawn-restricted. Study how this mod handles item flagging and spawn pools.

--- STILL UNCONFIRMED ---

STEAM 3622146187 — Name/features unconfirmed from research. Please provide
  the name if you remember it.

--- REFERENCE LIBRARIES / NOT HARD DEPENDENCIES ---

Nexus 411 — Calradia at War / CustomSpawns API
  Open source on GitHub: github.com/CustomSpawnsTeam/CustomSpawns
  Our sub-faction dynamic state system studies this architecture. Do not mark
  it as a required dependency until source/API compatibility is verified.

Nexus 9564 — No More Idle Minor Factions
  All new sub-factions MUST have explicit home settlement assignment in C# or
  they may drift or behave incorrectly. The Diathma drift claim is a known
  modding warning, not locally proven here as a universal engine bug. The
  non-negotiable part is explicit home settlement and runtime validation.

Nexus 3015 — Religions mod
  Devotion system per character and settlement. Reference for Undying Flame
  devotion-spread escalation system. Not a dependency unless source audit and
  v1.4.5/War Sails compatibility are proven.


--- RESEARCH CANDIDATES — PENDING DEEP ANALYSIS ---

CRITICAL WARNING FOR CLAUDE CODE:
Every entry below is identified from mod descriptions, changelogs, and surface
research only. None of it has been verified against actual source code.
Descriptions lie. Changelogs omit. Compatibility claims are often wrong.

Before extracting ANY feature or approach from these mods, Claude Code must:
  1. Read the actual source code, not the description
  2. Verify the feature works as described in the current War Sails version
  3. Check for conflicts with Bannerwake's custom culture/troop/faction systems
  4. Determine whether the implementation approach is actually what we assumed
  5. Document findings in MISSING_ASSETS.md or a dedicated RESEARCH_NOTES.md

These are hypotheses about useful features. Treat them as starting points for
investigation, not confirmed implementation paths.

NEXUS 832 — Diplomacy
  Identified features (UNVERIFIED): Messengers, Alliances, Non-Aggression Pacts,
    War Exhaustion tracker, civil war mechanics (secession, forced abdication),
    vassal diplomacy control, expansionism penalties, influence balancing.
  Why we care: War exhaustion may back our faction event mechanics (Boyar
    Council, Feudal Obligation, Imperial Legitimacy). Civil war/secession
    mechanic may be the implementation path for Karakhergit and Beni Zilal
    breaking from their parent factions. Messenger system may inform our
    world notification design.
  What Claude Code must verify: Does it actually work with War Sails? Does
    the civil war system use ChangeOwnerOfSettlement in ways we can adapt?
    Is the war exhaustion exposed as a queryable C# variable?

NEXUS 8427 — WarAndAiTweaks
  Identified features (UNVERIFIED): Full Military AI replacement, anti-snowball
    coalition logic, marshal system, non-linear war fatigue, narrative flavor
    text on war declarations, army cooperation and objective pursuit.
  Why we care: Anti-snowball is critical — if one Empire faction steamrolls,
    the others must react. The marshal system may be the reference for Boyar
    Council and Grove Council appointing war leaders. Narrative flavor text
    on war declarations is the architecture for our faction-specific war events.
  What Claude Code must verify: Does it conflict with our custom faction
    behavior? Can the coalition logic be extracted without the full AI
    replacement? Is it War Sails compatible? Does the marshal system use
    any mechanics we can adapt for sub-faction leader behavior?

NEXUS 10609 — Bellum Civile
  Identified features (UNVERIFIED): Internal faction politics, rebellious intent
    tracking, rebel faction formation, civil war system, BC marriage alliances,
    policy agendas, detailed rebellion UI.
  Why we care: Rebellious intent tracking is the technical reference for
    Raganvad's precarious position — boyars who lost enough battles show
    intent. Rebel faction formation is the path for Beni Zilal and Karakhergit
    escalation. Civil war system informs Imperial Legitimacy event design.
  What Claude Code must verify: This is a complex mod. Read the source
    thoroughly. How does it track rebellious intent? What events does it
    hook into? Is it compatible with custom minor faction behavior? Can
    individual systems be extracted cleanly without the full mod?

NEXUS 791 — Realistic Battle Mod (RBM) + War Sails submod
  Identified features (UNVERIFIED): Formation-based combat AI, armor
    penetration based on weapon type, armor weak-spots, improved defender
    positions, spear buffs, formation depth. War Sails submod adds Nord
    overhaul, mariner troop handling.
  Why we care: Bannerwake's combat identity pillar requires that faction
    differences feel real in battle — not just in unit names but in how
    fights actually play out. A Sturgian shieldwall should feel different
    from a Khuzait swarm. Without proper formation AI this lives only in
    visual design. RBM may be the closest existing implementation.
  What Claude Code must verify: The War Sails submod specifically — how
    does it handle Nord overhaul and mariner troops? Does it conflict with
    Bannerwake's custom troop trees? Can the formation AI be used without
    the full combat damage overhaul? Is the armor system compatible with our
    visual-identity-first armor approach?

NEXUS 639 — DynaCulture
  Identified features (UNVERIFIED): Dynamic settlement culture influence
    based on owning kingdom. Gradual assimilation with configurable resistance.
    Updates notables, recruitment, and ship pools after conversion. External
    save system.
  Why we care: The Undying Flame devotion spreading through Imperial settlements
    is mechanically similar to what this mod describes — influence radiating
    outward from controlled territory. The algorithm for calculating spread
    rate based on surrounding influence may be directly adaptable.
  What Claude Code must verify: How exactly does the influence calculation
    work? Is it daily tick based? Can the "culture" concept be replaced with
    a "devotion" concept in our implementation? Does it update notables in
    ways we can hook into for Flame-converted settlement NPCs?

NEXUS 9675 — Cultural Identity
  Identified features (UNVERIFIED): Lords automatically purchase culture-
    appropriate equipment. Settlement cultural conversion with loyalty-based
    speed. Notable conversion tracking. Retroactive application.
  Why we care: Lord visual identity is a Phase 7 requirement. An Aserai lord
    should look Aserai. A Sturgian lord should look Sturgian. This mod
    describes exactly that behavior for NPCs.
  What Claude Code must verify: Does it conflict with our custom armor
    assignment system? Does the equipment restriction work correctly with
    modded item pools from OSA and Swadian Armoury? Can the settlement
    conversion be configured to work alongside our sub-faction dynamic state
    system without conflicts?

NEXUS 3826 — Banner Kings
  Identified features (UNVERIFIED): Population system, feudal titles, village
    projects, settlement management, education, languages, religions framework,
    court system, shipping routes, economy rebalancing, cultural equipment
    restrictions, troop spawn system.
  Why we care: The religion framework is the most mature implementation of
    devotion-spread in Bannerlord modding. The shipping routes may inform
    our port economy design. The court system informs our Boyar Council
    and feudal lord mechanics.
  CRITICAL NOTE: Banner Kings explicitly conflicts with custom troop trees
    and faction mods. DO NOT use Banner Kings as a dependency or copy its
    systems wholesale. Study specific sub-systems only. The religions
    framework source code is the primary interest — read it, understand the
    architecture, build our own lighter version.
  What Claude Code must verify: Read the religions sub-system specifically.
    How does devotion spread per settlement? How does it update NPC behavior?
    Is the source available on GitHub for study?


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 8b: CONFIRMED DESIGN CHANGES FROM STATE SYSTEM SESSION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The following changes were confirmed during the sub-faction state system design
session. They supersede any earlier entries in this document.

CANONICAL FACTION LOCKS:
  THE RENEGATI
    Culture: Imperial. Renegade Imperial naval officers who kept their ships
    when the Empire fractured. They denied their oath and kept their galleys.
    Now they tax the southern sea lanes they used to protect.
    Leader: Route-Master Cassius Vorn (Imperial name, bureaucratic title)
    Visual: Imperial naval armor, recognizably Imperial but no longer
    polished. Rust maintained rather than removed. They look like the Empire
    used to look. Just angrier and less supervised.

  THE AKRITOI
    Culture: Imperial frontier cavalry mercenaries.
    Real Byzantine term for frontier border warriors.
    Pure elite frontier cavalry. No intelligence-network concept.
    Leader: Archon Demetrios of the Steppe.

  THE LAST LEGION / THE OLD LEGION
    Before integration: Last Legion.
    After Dragon Banner / restored Calradic Empire integration: The Old Legion.
    Not restored. Not reborn. Always the Legion.

  FORBIDDEN OLD CODE NAMES:
    Skolvard, Tidewrack, Ravenflock, Wildborn, Ironblood.
    These are retired design-history names and must not appear in XML IDs,
    C# classes, folders, file names, state-machine headings, or UI strings.
    Eleftheroi is NOT a forbidden XML/source id. It is an actual local minor
    faction id and the closest implementation hook for Akritoi. It is forbidden
    only as the current Bannerwake display name for that concept.

JAWWAL TROOP TREE (confirmed):
  Pure camel faction — NO horses at any tier.
  Camel mounts implemented using existing Bannerlord camel mount system.
  Camel tiers scale with unit tier: basic camels at T1-T2, War Camels at
  T3-T4, finest camels at T5.
  Camel horse-fear mechanic: flag as RESEARCH TASK for Claude Code.
  May be implementable via C# morale patch — requires investigation.
  Do not assume it works without verifying.

  Jawwal Ghazw (T1) — on foot or basic camel, barely armed, light linen
  Jawwal Farsan (T2) — camel rider, javelin + short sword, minimal armor
  Jawwal Sahrawiy (T3) — experienced raider, bow + blade, light armor
  Jawwal Hamlah (T4) — war camel, lance + scimitar, medium armor
  Jawwal Sha'ir (T5) — finest war camel, best weapons, light armor by choice.
    The poet-warrior. Recites verse before battle. Genuinely dangerous.
    Sha'ir = poet in Arabic.

LAST LEGION / DRAGON BANNER INTEGRATION:
  Last Legion holds a randomly selected Imperial border castle from campaign
  start. Garrison is programmatically inflated — AI patched to deprioritize
  sieging it until a later campaign stage.
  Quest integration:
    Arzagos path (destroy Empire): Last Legion turns HOSTILE to player
      specifically. They pursue the player with dedicated parties. Personal
      and permanent.
    Istiana path (restore Empire): Last Legion shifts to WATCHFUL. Slightly
      favorable. They don't attack the player. They wait for proof.
    2/3 Imperial territory controlled + Istiana path: Messenger event fires.
      One meeting offered. Player earns trust through actions, not words.
    Full integration event: parchment card fires. They offer their castle
      and troops. Name changes to The Old Legion.
    No quest path: Last Legion remains in default neutral-to-hostile state.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 9: SUB-FACTION DYNAMIC STATE SYSTEM — FULL SPECIFICATION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This is the highest-risk technical system in Bannerwake. Build it as a skeleton
first. Test it empty. Then populate with faction behaviors one at a time.
Every other system in Bannerwake queries this one. It is the spine.

ARCHITECTURE:
  Hybrid approach — one global manager + individual faction behavior classes.
  bw_SubFactionStateManager: the single queryable source of truth. Every other
    system asks this class what state a sub-faction is in. Never query
    individual faction classes directly from outside systems.
  bw_[FactionName]StateBehavior: each sub-faction has its own class that
    defines its transition logic. Clean separation. Black Keel's logic cannot
    accidentally affect Grovebound's logic.
  State enum: DORMANT / ACTIVE / ESCALATED / CRISIS
  Transition engine: CONDITIONS unlock escalation possibility.
    TIME drives it forward if unchecked.
  De-escalation: some sub-factions can be pushed back, others cannot.

PERMANENCE CATEGORIES:
  PERMANENT factions (cannot be eliminated through normal clan destruction):
    Black Keel — persistent pseudo-kingdom/custom-kingdom threat. Verify the
    correct local v1.4.5 clan/kingdom elimination hook before patching.
    Ironjarls — permanent Nord political dissident faction, not mercenary.
  SUPPRESSIBLE factions (can be pushed back, re-emerge if conditions return):
    All other sub-factions. Defeat sets them back. Conditions bring them back.

--- BLACK KEEL STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  DORMANT → ACTIVE: Pure fixed timer. Fires regardless of world conditions.
    Hrothgar and all captains hidden in fog of war throughout DORMANT.
    No parties. No presence. Player has no idea they exist.
    Looter-style small parties (15-40 troops) begin spawning on northern
    coasts using the bandit spawn system when ACTIVE fires.
    Lord parties do NOT appear yet during ACTIVE.
  ACTIVE → ESCALATED: Second fixed timer after going ACTIVE.
    Lord parties emerge. Named captains roam northern coast and sea lanes.
    Behave like minor faction lords. Grow in size over time.
    Hrothgar STILL hidden. World notification parchment card fires.
  ESCALATED → CRISIS: Third fixed timer.
    Hrothgar reveals himself. World event fires.
    Black Keel seizes HVALVIK specifically via a Bannerwake wrapper around
    ChangeOwnerOfSettlementAction.
    Garrison set to approximately 2,000 troops programmatically.
    AI patched to prevent casual NPC siege of Hvalvik.
    Hrothgar's party appears at Hvalvik.
    Settlement becomes Black Keel stronghold: their culture, their troops.
  RECONSTITUTION: If defeated at any state, lord parties go quiet for
    recovery period then re-emerge. Looter-style spawns NEVER fully stop.
    If Hvalvik retaken, resets to ESCALATED. Timer restarts. They return.
  SPAWN SYSTEM: Two parallel systems running simultaneously from ACTIVE:
    1. Looter-style spawns: 15-40 troops, bandit spawn system, always present
    2. Lord-style parties: named captain clans, minor faction lord behavior

--- LAKE RATS STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  DORMANT → ACTIVE: Early campaign timer. Small wrecker parties on Lake
    Laconis. Harbormaster not visible.
  ACTIVE → ESCALATED: Player-inactivity timer. If no action taken against
    Lake Rats in X days, formal toll operation fires. Any ship on Lake Laconis
    pays tribute or is attacked.
  CAP: Lake Rats cap at ESCALATED. No CRISIS state. Regional criminal
    operation, not a world threat.
  DE-ESCALATION: Player can destroy the beacon network through a specific
    quest. Pushes back to ACTIVE. They rebuild beacons over time. Returns to
    ESCALATED if ignored again.

--- JOMSVIKINGR STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  Always ACTIVE from campaign start. Mercenary brotherhood.
  ACTIVE → ESCALATED: Nordvyg loses significant territory (tracked by C#
    variable). Jomsvikingr declares independence, fortifies their hall as
    permanent independent entity.
  No CRISIS state. Political escalation only, not a threat arc.

--- GROVEBOUND STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  DORMANT → ACTIVE: Early-mid campaign. Groveseers begin appearing as
    traveling NPCs. Fighter parties appear in deep Battanian forest.
  ACTIVE → ESCALATED: Sacred site disturbed OR Battanian territory loss
    begins. Parties grow. Groveseers more visible.
  ESCALATED → CRISIS (THE GATHERING): Fires when Battania crosses a critical
    threshold — too many settlements lost OR a sacred site fully destroyed.
    Overwhelming temporary wave descends on responsible party/settlement.
    Returns to ESCALATED after the wave passes.
    THE GATHERING IS BATTANIA'S EXISTENTIAL DEFENSE MECHANISM.
    Fires multiple times per campaign — as many times as the threshold is
    crossed. Minimum cooldown between Gatherings prevents spam.
    The old gods don't let Battania die. This is mechanically enforced.
    Gathering Warriors do NOT appear in normal spawn pools. Event only.

--- KARAKHERGIT STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  DORMANT → ACTIVE: Fires when Khuzait begins losing territory (C# variable
    tracking Khuzait settlement count).
  ACTIVE: Specifically targets Khuzait caravans. Neutral to all other factions.
  ESCALATED: If Khuzait loses enough territory, Karakhergit declare ancestral
    range claim and begin formal reclamation campaign.
  CRISIS/RESOLUTION: If player gives them a stronghold, reconstitute as
    Khanate of Karak. Full political faction.

--- WOODSMEN OF ROBYNN STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  DORMANT → ACTIVE: Very early campaign. Discovery event fires within first
    30 days. Robynn is already operating in Forrath forest.
  ACTIVE: Raid noble convoys only. Never attack villages. Custom AiBehavior
    patch suppresses RaidSettlement, only lord parties and noble caravans
    as valid targets.
  NO CORRUPTION ARC. Robynn stays heroic throughout. The legend doesn't
    collapse. There are hiccups. The heroism is real. So is the mess.

--- COMPANY OF THE GOLDEN BOAR STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  Always ACTIVE from campaign start. Discovery event fires early.
  Mercenary faction. Available to hire.
  ESCALATED: Powerful and unpaid long enough. Captain-General demands land
    as payment instead of gold.
  CRISIS: If betrayed, accepts a contract specifically against the betrayer.
    The memory system is the spine of their entire state logic.
    Every interaction logged permanently in C# tracked variable.

--- UNDYING FLAME STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  DORMANT → ACTIVE: Small hidden cells. No visible presence early game.
    Parties begin appearing in Imperial territory. Villages report burnings.
  ACTIVE → ESCALATED: Devotion spreads through settlements via DynaCulture-
    inspired algorithm after source audit. This is a custom Bannerwake
    devotion tracker, not a direct dependency. Three simultaneous spread
    multipliers:
      1. Poverty/prosperity: economically weak settlements more susceptible
      2. Military trauma: recently raided/sieged settlements convert faster
      3. Proximity: settlements adjacent to converted settlements pulled in
    All three stack simultaneously.
  ESCALATED → CRISIS (THE VESSEL): Fires when ANY Imperial town (not village)
    falls to Flame devotion. The Vessel is elected. Fourth Empire declared.
    The moment the cult penetrates real Imperial urban power, they believe
    the time has come. One town is enough.
  Fire system: Reference Nexus 4547 + Steam 3542272439. Build unified
    bw_FireWeaponBehavior C# class. Culture tag check: only bw_undying_flame
    triggers fire effects. Fire exclusive to Undying Flame.

--- LAST LEGION STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  Always ACTIVE from campaign start. Holds one random Imperial border castle.
  Garrison inflated. AI patched against casual siege.
  WATCHFUL (pseudo-state): Istiana path completed. They observe the player.
  INTEGRATION EVENT: Istiana path + 2/3 Imperial territory controlled.
    Name changes to The Old Legion. Castle offered. Troops available.
  HOSTILE: Arzagos path completed. Pursue player specifically. Personal.
  See Section 8b for full Dragon Banner quest integration details.

--- HIDDEN HAND STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  Always ACTIVE from campaign start. No formal discovery event.
  Player notices something wrong. That IS the introduction.
  CRISIS A (Exposure): Player gets too close to dismantling their network.
    They strike back through proxies — hired parties, false accusations,
    relationship hits with lords on their payroll. Never directly.
  CRISIS B (Flame Threat): Undying Flame reaches ESCALATED. The Hand moves
    against the Flame. They approach the player as a potential tool.
  Both CRISIS states can fire simultaneously and independently.

--- AKRITOI STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  DORMANT → ACTIVE: Early campaign. Discovery event fires within first 30
    days. Pure frontier cavalry mercenaries. Hireable.
  ESCALATED: Intelligence about frontier movements available through quest
    chains. They know where things are.
  No CRISIS state.

--- JAWWAL STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  Always ACTIVE from campaign start. Discovery event fires early.
  Desert nomads. Always in the Nahhas desert. Always have been.
  ESCALATED: Aserai military collapses. Jawwal expand dramatically. Claim
    oasis settlements. Become genuine desert power.
  Pure camel faction throughout. See Section 8b for full troop tree.

--- GHILMAN STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  Always ACTIVE from campaign start. Hireable mercenaries.
  THE GHILMAN RULE: Never fight other Ghilman. Contract voids immediately
    if Ghilman-vs-Ghilman confrontation detected. C# contract monitoring
    behavior. No exceptions. Must function correctly.
  No escalation states. Professional faction.

--- BENI ZILAL STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  DORMANT → ACTIVE: Aserai throne instability rises through military losses.
  ACTIVE → ESCALATED: Formally declare their claimant. Stop raiding, start
    campaigning.
  ESCALATED → CRISIS: Aserai loses enough territory. Attempt to seize an
    Aserai settlement via a Bannerwake wrapper around
    ChangeOwnerOfSettlementAction.

--- DUST SPEAKERS STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  Always present but universally neutral. No formal ACTIVE state.
  CRISIS: Burial ground disturbed (deliberately or through ignorance).
    Become PERMANENT IMPLACABLE ENEMY. Never forgive. Never de-escalate.

--- THE RENEGATI STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  DORMANT → ACTIVE: Early campaign. Discovery event fires within first 30
    days. Southern sea lane toll operation already running.
  ACTIVE: Not openly hostile. They board ships, take percentage, let them
    sail on. Calculating, businesslike. No violence unless refused.
  ESCALATED: Player-adjacent trade routes actively raided if player hasn't
    paid or attacked them.
  CRISIS: If player attacks them consistently, they accept a contract against
    player-adjacent trade. Full naval harassment.
  DE-ESCALATION: Player can pay tribute or establish a formal arrangement.
    Pushes back to ACTIVE. Businesslike, not personal.

--- THE SHROUD STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  Always ACTIVE. Faces embedded as normal NPCs from campaign start.
  Fragment system running passively throughout campaign.
  No discovery event — they don't announce themselves. The player notices
    something wrong. That IS the introduction.
  ACTIVE → ESCALATED: Player power threshold crossed. Player has become a
    significant enough force in Calradia to matter. First contact fires.
    A Face approaches. Demonstrates omniscience. Offers something minor.
    Veil Riders begin appearing on the steppe and northern coast.
  ESCALATED → CRISIS (THE SHROUD REVEALED): Enough fragments assembled AND
    Veil Riders encountered. The picture coheres. The Unnamed Fleet appears
    on the coastal map. The player now knows what is coming.
    The game never tells them what to do about it.
  THE SHROUD IS THE VILLAIN. Full ideological commitment at every tier.
    Every Face chose this. The Reader knows exactly what this is.
    NEVER give a definitive answer about The Shroud, The Reader, the Unnamed
    Fleet, or the ancient civilization anywhere in the mod.

--- IRONJARLS STATE TRANSITIONS ---
  Always ACTIVE from campaign start. Discovery event fires within first 30
    days — not a threat, just a presence. A name in a tavern.
  ACTIVE: Politically restless. Raid Halthdar's tax collectors and supply
    convoys specifically. Not villages. Not travelers. Political statement.
  ACTIVE → ESCALATED: Nordvyg loses significant territory (C# variable
    tracking Nord settlement count). Grimulf openly recruits. Parties grow.
    World notification: "The old names are being spoken again in the north."
  ESCALATED → CRISIS: Nordvyg loses enough. Grimulf declares old kingdoms
    restored. Seizes a specific Nord inland settlement via
    ChangeOwnerOfSettlementAction wrapper. Garrison inflated. He holds it as
    proof.
  DE-ESCALATION: Suppressible. Nordvyg recovers militarily → Ironjarls
    return to ESCALATED then ACTIVE. They go quiet when Halthdar looks strong.
    They never disappear entirely.
  RELATIONSHIP WITH PLAYER: A player who defeats Halthdar in battle earns
    Grimulf's attention. A player who helps Nordvyg earns his quiet hostility.
    He never shows either openly.

--- VRAK THE FOUL — RAT-KING OF VLANDIA ---
  NOT a campaign event. A full anti-Vlandian evil faction with settlements.
  Kingdom name: Mortia. Single-word, Vlandian-adjacent, wrong in the mouth,
    not a noble house name. Mortia is what Vrak calls the seized territory and
    what frightened merchants learn to write on ledgers when they cannot call
    it Vlandia anymore.
  Title: Rat-King Vrak the Foul
  Culture: Vlandian — the dark mirror of everything Vlandia pretends to be

  ORIGIN:
    Former high-ranking Vlandian military officer. Not a grunt — someone who
    understood logistics, supply chains, fortress vulnerabilities, troop
    movement. Corrupt from the inside. Got caught embezzling supply convoys.
    Sentenced to execution. Was being transported when a Woodsmen of Robynn
    ambush hit the convoy — chaos, burning wagons, guards routing. Vrak walked
    away. He owes his life to Robynn of the Wood without Robynn knowing or
    intending it. The Woodsmen of Robynn inadvertently created the Rat-King.
    Built his organization using military knowledge — the exact supply routes
    and garrison weaknesses he spent twenty years defending, now weaponized.

  FACTION IDENTITY:
    The dark mirror of Vlandia. Where Vlandia is gallant, radiant, knightly —
    Vrak is foul, calculating, and shameless. Same territory, same cultural
    roots, completely inverted values. His men don't follow him out of loyalty
    or admiration. They follow because he pays, because he's never lost yet,
    and because they're more afraid of what he'd do if they left than what his
    enemies would do if they stayed. He is a bastard who won. That's what
    makes him dangerous. Not a campaign event — he holds a small number of
    Vlandian border settlements from early campaign, expanding when Vlandia
    is distracted. Every castle he holds has a tunnel out.

  PERSONALITY — THE RAT-KING: STRONGHOLD + SKAVEN CALIBRATION:
    Primary references:
      Stronghold Crusader's Duc de Puce — The Rat. Hysterical, twitchy,
      arrogant that collapses when threatened, loud then suddenly quiet.
      Skaven from Total War: Warhammer — the underlying psychology without
      the fantasy vocabulary. Paranoid, self-aggrandizing, takes credit for
      everything, blames others for everything, convinced everyone is plotting
      against him (in Vrak's case this is probably accurate).
    Vrak is NOT honest. He is a Rat. He lies, deflects, takes credit for
    things he didn't do, blames others for things he did. What makes him
    different from Vlandian nobility is not honesty — it's that his
    dishonesty is naked and functional rather than dressed up as virtue.
    His men find this preferable to the alternative. Slightly.
    Hysterical, loud, ill-tempered underneath a calculating exterior.
    Arrogant about his intelligence, deeply insecure about everything else.
    Jittery, twitchy — the body betrays what the voice tries to conceal.
    Stutter appears specifically under pressure — forced to admit weakness,
    genuinely threatened, when the calculation slips and the anger shows.
    Finds things funny at wrong moments. Laughs when nothing is funny.
    Never raises his voice when genuinely threatening. Goes very quiet.
    That silence is the tell. When Vrak goes quiet, someone is about to die.
    Refers to Vlandian nobility as "those people" — contemptuous, personal.
    Never by title. Calls everyone "friend" when he means the opposite.
    Military vocabulary slips through occasionally. He catches it. That's
    more unsettling than if he didn't.
    He can't stop talking. Talking is how he controls a room. Silence makes
    him nervous. He is the most talkative named character in Bannerwake.
    Takes credit compulsively — if something succeeded, he planned it.
    Deflects blame compulsively — if something failed, someone else did it.
    Paranoid certainty that everyone is plotting against him at all times.
    His dialogue captures Skaven psychology in human speech:
      "I planned that. I planned— I planned all of that. The timing, the—
      the approach, the whole— yes. My plan. Hund suggested the flanking
      movement but that was— I had already thought of that. He just said it
      first. Because I told him to think about flanking. So. My plan."
      "You think— mmm. You think I don't know what you're doing? I know—
      I know what you're doing. I know what everyone is doing. I've always
      known. That's why I'm— [twitches] — that's why I'm still here and
      they're not."
    He is not admirable. He is effective. There is a difference. His men
    follow him because he keeps them alive, fed, and paid — not because
    they respect him. Sir Hund dey Brunval follows him not because Vrak
    is honest but because Vrak's dishonesty is at least legible. You can
    see Vrak's lies forming. That's better than noble lies you can't see.

  VISUAL IDENTITY — MILITARY THEATRICAL BANDIT:
    Helmet: Hound bascinet retextured — nose slightly rose-pink with engraved
      whiskers on the visor. Rat facial features worked into the metal.
      Not obvious at distance. Wrong up close. The player fights him and
      thinks about the helmet afterward. The player never sees his face.
    Armor: Old Vlandian officer armor, heraldry scratched off completely.
      Trophies added over years — pauldron from a lord he killed, cloak in
      no faction's color. Looks almost legitimate from distance. Up close
      every element has been modified. He erased himself from the record
      and built something new over the top of it.
    Banner: Brown and yellow. No noble device. Just color. The banner of
      men who wanted a banner.
    Silhouette: Military theatrical bandit. Officer's posture, officer's
      bearing, criminal's eyes. The contrast is deeply unsettling.

  ARMY COMPOSITION — HALF ATTEMPTING TO BE MILITARY:
    Outer layer — the mob: Standard looters and forest bandits. No loyalty
      beyond Vrak's presence. Cannon fodder who die cheap and replace cheap.
    Middle layer — criminal specialists: Mountain bandits, deserters, former
      soldiers who made worse choices than Vrak. Equipment, experience, hold
      a line when told. Not elite. Competent and committed to surviving.
    Inner core — personal guard: His officer knowledge applied to selection
      and training. Best looted equipment, fight like soldiers, personally
      loyal to Vrak specifically. When Vrak is defeated and driven out they
      scatter immediately — loyal to him, not the cause.
    The army looks organized at distance. Up close: chaos with structure
    underneath. When the veteran core breaks the whole thing dissolves.

  MORTIA KINGDOM STRUCTURE — FOUR CLANS:
    Mortia has 4 clans — Vrak's ruling clan and 3 vassals. Small but
    real. Enough to hold territory, not enough to be a major power. Every
    castle holds a tunnel out. This is the first thing built, not the last.

    CLAN 1 — VRAK'S RULING CLAN:
      Rat-King Vrak the Foul (leader)
      Holds: GALEND (town) + DRAPAND CASTLE
        Galend seized from Derthert dey Meroc directly — the king's own city.
        Drapand Castle seized from Aldric dey Tihr — his personal holding.
      SIGNIFICANCE: Taking Galend from Derthert is not just territorial —
        it is a specific humiliation. The king whose city was taken by a rat.
        Derthert cannot ignore this. It defines his entire reign's legacy.
        Aldric lost Drapand and is furious in the specific way of a man
        who had something taken — not embarrassed, just enraged.

    CLAN 2 — DEY VORNAC (the exiled noble):
      Baron Aldmar dey Vornac (leader)
      Background: Former Vlandian baron stripped of title through court
        politics. Lost land to a rival he couldn't outmaneuver in council.
        Too proud to serve anyone legitimate, too weak to fight back alone.
        Came to Vrak because Vrak was the only man who would give him a
        castle without demanding he kneel first.
      Relationship with Vrak: Open contempt. Professionally useful contempt.
        Aldmar knows every weakness in Vlandian court politics — who owes
        whom, which baron's books have the same gaps Vrak's did, which lords
        attended the trial and looked the other way. Vrak finds this useful.
        Aldmar despises being useful to Vrak. Vrak finds this additionally
        useful. A man who hates you and serves you anyway is the most
        reliable kind of servant.
      Holds: TALIVEL CASTLE [castle_V7] (seized from Derthert dey Meroc)
        Talivel was also a ruling clan holding. Aldmar holds two of
        Derthert's former assets under Vrak's banner. Derthert finds
        this specific combination personally insulting.
      Visual: Still dresses like a Vlandian baron. The heraldry is his
        original heraldry, not Vrak's brown and yellow. Vrak allows this.
        It costs nothing. The baron's dignity keeps him functional.

    CLAN 3 — DEY BRACKFEN (the knighted smuggler):
      Sir Gurn dey Brackfen (leader)
      Background: Career smuggler who controlled trade routes through
        Vlandian forests before Vrak was even a fugitive. When Vrak escaped
        into those same forests, Gurn was already operating there. They met
        eventually — the forest was smaller than either expected. Vrak needed
        the routes. Gurn needed protection and scale. Arrangement was obvious.
      Vrak knighted him. Sir Gurn dey Brackfen. In a courtyard full of
        criminals. A sword on a smuggler's shoulder. Gurn tells this story
        in taverns. The audience is never sure whether to laugh or be unsettled.
        Both, usually. He finds his title useful for intimidation and nothing
        else. Dey Brackfen is a holding Vrak invented when he gave him the
        castle. It didn't have that name before.
      Relationship with Vrak: Serves the arrangement, not the man. As long
        as the arrangement pays better than alternatives, he stays. He's been
        making that calculation his entire career. The day it resolves against
        Vrak, he'll disappear into the forest the same way he appeared.
        Vrak knows this. Keeps him paid. Keeps the routes running.
      Holds: JACULAN [town_V6] (seized from Calatild dey Arromanc)
        Calatild lost Jaculan to a smuggler Vrak knighted on a whim.
        Ingalther dey Cortain has been pressing for Jaculan for years.
        He is quietly satisfied Calatild doesn't have it. He is less
        satisfied that Vrak does. Both feelings exist simultaneously.
      Value to the faction: The Greypaths — unmarked forest routes that
        aren't on any lord's map. Information, supply, escape routes.
        Vrak's entire re-emergence capability depends on these routes.

    CLAN 4 — DEY BRUNVAL (the military reject):
      Sir Hund dey Brunval (leader)
      Background: Former Vlandian sergeant discharged for excessive brutality
        against prisoners. Did things on campaign his officers had quietly
        approved before — did them in front of the wrong people at the wrong
        time in a way that required documentation. His officers wrote the
        report because they had to. Didn't look him in the eye when they
        handed him his discharge. He noticed. He always notices that gap.
      Vrak knighted him. Sir Hund. In a ceremony that was theatrical and
        slightly wrong — Vrak conducting it with military bearing, courtyard
        full of criminals standing at attention, a sword on a kneeling
        sergeant's shoulder. He tells nobody about this. The title is useful.
      Relationship with Vrak: Follows because Vrak's dishonesty is legible.
        You can see Vrak's lies forming. Better than noble lies you can't see.
        Not loyalty — legibility. He can track what Vrak is doing. That's
        enough. He is the most genuinely dangerous man in Vrak's kingdom and
        Vrak knows it. He keeps Hund close and fed and never turns his back.
      Holds: HONGARD CASTLE [castle_V2] (the most defensible, the ugliest,
        fits its owner)

    CLAN 1 — VRAK'S RULING CLAN (personal guard enforcers):
      Leader: Rat-King Vrak the Foul
      Holds: 1 town + 1 castle
      Members (6 total):
        Mordric — Enforcer. Vrak's closest shadow. Does what Vrak won't do
          in front of witnesses.
        Halvec — Quartermaster. Manages Vrak's supply chain. The same role
          Vrak had in Vlandia, now criminal. He's very good at it.
        Dunara — Scout. The only woman in Vrak's inner circle. Vrak trusts
          her precisely because nobody suspects her.
        Brecholt — Gate captain. Holds Vrak's castle gates. Has never let
          anyone through he shouldn't. Has never explained how he decides.
        Garven — Treasurer. Counts every coin. Vrak checks his work twice.
          Garven knows this. Counts correctly.
      Naming: Invented harsh Germanic compounds. Dark and functional.

    CLAN 2 — DEY VORNAC (exiled noble household remnant):
      Leader: Baron Aldmar dey Vornac
      Holds: TALIVEL CASTLE [castle_V7]
      Members (6 total):
        Elthara dey Vornac — Wife. Followed him into exile. Has never
          complained about it. Has never forgiven him for it either.
        Rendric dey Vornac — Son (elder). Young, angry, blames his father
          for everything. Not wrong.
        Malgund dey Vornac — Son (younger). Quieter than Rendric. More
          dangerous than Rendric. Aldmar doesn't know this yet.
        Sir Halvorn — The one loyal knight who followed Aldmar out.
          Old now. Still fights. Doesn't know what else to do.
        Dunfeld — Household steward. Managed Aldmar's estate for twenty
          years. Followed because the estate was gone and he had nothing
          else to manage. Finds the criminal environment upsetting.
      Naming: Legitimate Norman-French Vlandian names. Sound like they
        belong at Derthert's court. Wrong address now.

    CLAN 3 — DEY BRACKFEN (smuggling network lieutenants):
      Leader: Sir Gurn dey Brackfen
      Holds: JACULAN [town_V6]
      Members (6 total):
        Vorda — Route-keeper. Knows every Greypath. Gurn's most valuable
          person. Not a fighter — a navigator. Vrak has considered having
          her specifically protected above all other clan members.
        Brek dey Brackfen — Fighter. Gurn's muscle. Vrak knighted him too
          in the same ceremony. He uses the title to get better rooms at
          inns and finds this the most useful thing about it.
        Cralt — Informant. Placed in three Vlandian towns before Vrak
          seized the castle. Still there. Reports monthly. Has a family
          in one of those towns that doesn't know what he does.
        Dunwick — Fence. Moves everything that comes through the Greypaths.
          Knows the price of everything in Vlandia. Has never been caught.
          Has a theory about why. Doesn't share the theory.
        Ashe dey Brackfen — Gurn's daughter. Did not choose this life.
          Is very good at it anyway. Gurn is proud of this and guilty
          about being proud of it.
      Naming: Short rough names. Some dey Brackfen suffix for those Vrak
        formally knighted. Functional, criminal, no pretense.

    CLAN 4 — DEY BRUNVAL (military rejects):
      Leader: Sir Hund dey Brunval
      Holds: HONGARD CASTLE [castle_V2] (most defensible, ugliest, fits its owner)
      Members (6 total):
        Varnec — Second in command. Was discharged the same day as Hund
          for the same reasons. They don't discuss it. Have never discussed
          it. Understand each other completely.
        Boltric — Siege specialist. Knows how to break walls. Knows how
          to break other things. Vlandia taught him both. Was considered
          an asset until the same thing that makes him an asset became
          a liability.
        Margund — Field surgeon. Stopped following Vlandian rules about
          when to save someone and when not to. Made the wrong call once
          in front of the wrong people. Now works for Hund.
        Dravold — Trainer. Drills Hund's men like soldiers. Because they
          are soldiers. Just not anyone's soldiers anymore. His drills are
          exactly regulation Vlandian military drills. This is unsettling.
        Grethild — Spy. Was placed inside a Vlandian garrison before Hund
          left service. Is still there. Sends letters. Nobody has connected
          her to Hund. Hund checks every letter for signs someone has.
      Naming: Military Germanic compounds. Sound like unit designations
        more than people. They would consider this a compliment.

  HERO NAMING NOTES FOR CLAUDE CODE:
    No name appears twice across all four clans — verified.
    Female members present in every clan for social plausibility, dialogue,
      family ties, and encyclopedia depth. Do NOT build birth-dependent
      succession around them; Bannerwake campaign mode assumes no birth/death.
    Vrak's clan names: harsh Germanic invented compounds
    Dey Vornac names: legitimate Norman-French, sound like Vlandia
    Dey Brackfen names: short, rough, some dey suffix for knighted members
    Dey Brunval names: military Germanic, sound like unit designations
    Total named heroes across all four clans: 24 heroes
    This is appropriate for a small criminal kingdom — enough to populate
      the map believably without inflating Vrak's faction beyond its
      actual territorial holdings.


      Vrak knights people because he can't and does anyway. He cannot grant
      noble titles by any legitimate standard — no king recognized him, no
      emperor legitimized him. But who's going to tell him that? The castle
      guards are his. The swords are his. Legitimacy is a convenience, not
      a requirement, when you hold the walls.
      Sir Gurn dey Brackfen (if encountered) finds his knighting hilarious.
      Baron Aldmar dey Vornac finds it deeply offensive.
      Sir Hund dey Brunval doesn't care either way. The title is useful.
      Vrak finds all three reactions satisfying for different reasons.


    Hundreds of authored lines. Conversations repeat rarely.
    Every topic has multiple variations based on:
      Player karma axes (Cunning players get a different Vrak than Honor)
      Current world state (his confidence varies with his faction's strength)
      Previous encounters (he remembers what was said)
      Player's faction (specific contempt for specific kingdoms)
    He is simultaneously the funniest and most unsettling NPC in Bannerwake.

  SAMPLE DIALOGUE (tone reference only — full lines authored separately):

  Player: "You were Vlandian once."
  Vrak: "Was. Yes. Mmm. They had— they had a different word for me then.
    Several words actually. Most of them rank. Some complimentary. Funny
    how that works. You serve twenty years and they decide one morning
    you're— what was it— 'an embarrassment to the office.' Ha. An
    embarrassment. I embezzled three supply convoys and they caught me on
    the fourth. The fourth! Twenty years and they caught me on the f-f-f—
    on the last one. No patience. No— no appreciation for the ratio."

  Player: "You could surrender."
  Vrak: "Mmm. Yes. I could. I c-could do that. Kneel in the mud, hands
    behind my head, very— very dignified. They'd hang me inside a week.
    No. No I don't think— I don't think I'll do that. But it's kind of
    you to offer. Very k-kind. You're a kind person. I can tell. The kind
    ones always come with the biggest armies. Funny. Funny how that works."

  Player: "Your men don't respect you."
  Vrak: [goes very quiet] "...No. No they don't. [long pause] They're
    alive though. Funny how that works too. The ones who respected their
    lords— where are they? Hmm? Where are the respectful ones? [twitches]
    Respect. Ha. Respect is what you give someone who can't make you afraid.
    My men are afraid. That lasts longer."

  STATE TRANSITIONS:
    ACTIVE: Holds settlements from early campaign. Discovery event fires.
    ESCALATED: Expands when Vlandia is distracted by war.
    CRISIS: Controls enough settlements to be a genuine regional threat.
    RE-EMERGENCE: If defeated and driven out, Vrak escapes. Always escapes.
      He escaped execution once. Given enough time and ignored bandit
      activity, he returns. The second notification the player reads carries
      recognition: "Word from the Vlandian roads. Someone has given the
      wolves a name again."

  PARCHMENT EVENT CARD (emergence):
    "The supply convoy from Galend arrived in Sargot three days late and
    forty percent short. The sergeant-at-arms says his men were hit on the
    Forrath road by what he took for a large bandit band — until he noticed
    they were moving in formation. Until he noticed the man giving orders
    hadn't panicked when the fighting started. Until he noticed that man
    wore a Vlandian officer's gorget with the heraldry scratched off. He
    says the man watched the whole thing from horseback without drawing his
    weapon. When it was over, the man rode forward and counted the wagons
    himself. Then he said something to the sergeant that the sergeant won't
